> AGENT

Here's the answer:

**No, `reflect backfill` does NOT re-analyze prompt quality.** It only re-extracts **facets** (friction points, effective patterns, session character, etc.).

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The codebase has **two completely separate LLM analysis pipelines**:

1. **Session insights + facets** (`analyzeSession()` at `analysis.ts:~170`) — generates insights (summary, key decisions, etc.) AND facets (friction points, patterns, attribution). The backfill path uses a lightweight variant, `extractFacetsOnly()` at `analysis.ts:882`, which only re-extracts facets using a stripped-down prompt (summary + first/last 20 messages).

2. **Prompt quality** (`analyzePromptQuality()` at `analysis.ts:289`) — a completely separate function with its own prompt (`generatePromptQualityPrompt`), its own save logic that explicitly targets only `prompt_quality` type insights, and its own delete logic (`includeOnlyTypes: ['prompt_quality']`).

Notice the careful isolation at save time: session analysis deletes old insights `excludeTypes: ['prompt_quality']` (line 252), while prompt quality deletes `includeOnlyTypes: ['prompt_quality']` (line 360). They never step on each other.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

So for your recent changes:

| Change | Covered by `reflect backfill`? | Re-analysis needed? |
|--------|-------------------------------|---------------------|
| Friction category taxonomy (PR #136, #138) | **Yes** — backfill re-extracts facets with updated prompts | Already handled |
| Pattern taxonomy/driver (PR #129, #138) | **Yes** — same facet extraction | Already handled |
| Prompt quality taxonomy (PR #136) | **No** — backfill doesn't touch PQ | **Needs separate re-analysis** |

To get updated prompt quality data for existing sessions, users would need to manually re-trigger prompt quality analysis per session (via the dashboard's "Analyze" button). There's no bulk PQ backfill path today.

**Options to consider:**
1. **Add PQ to the existing backfill flow** — extend `reflect backfill` to also re-run `analyzePromptQuality()` for sessions with outdated PQ data
2. **Separate `reflect backfill --prompt-quality` flag** — keep them independent but give users a way to bulk re-analyze PQ
3. **Do nothing** — PQ changes are additive enough that old data is still valid, just missing the new category normalization

Would you like to explore any of these approaches?

    11→function confirmPrompt(message: string): Promise<boolean> {

    45→        console.log(chalk.dim('  Configure it with: code-insights config llm'));

    54→
    55→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

    58→
    59→async function fetchWithSSE(url: string, body: Record<string, unknown>, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
    60→  const res = await fetch(url, {
    61→    method: 'POST',
    62→    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
    63→    body: JSON.stringify(body),
    64→    signal,
    65→  });
    66→
    67→  if (!res.ok) {
    68→    const text = await res.text().catch(() => res.statusText);
    69→    throw new Error(`Server error ${res.status}: ${text}`);
    70→  }
    71→
    72→  if (!res.body) throw new Error('No response body');
    73→
    74→  const reader = res.body.pipeThrough(new TextDecoderStream()).getReader();
    75→  let buffer = '';
    76→  let currentEvent = '';
    77→  let currentData = '';
    78→  let result: Record<string, unknown> = {};
    79→
    80→  const spinner = ora({ text: 'Starting...', indent: 2 }).start();
    81→
    82→  try {
    83→    while (true) {
    84→      const { done, value } = await reader.read();
    85→      if (done) break;
    86→
    87→      buffer += value;
    88→      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
    89→      buffer = lines.pop() ?? '';
    90→
    91→      for (const line of lines) {
    92→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    93→          currentEvent = line.slice(7).trim();
    94→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    95→          currentData = line.slice(6);
    96→        } else if (line === '' && currentEvent && currentData) {
    97→          try {
    98→            const data = JSON.parse(currentData) as Record<string, unknown>;
    99→
   100→            if (currentEvent === 'progress') {
   101→              spinner.text = (data.message as string) || 'Processing...';
   102→            } else if (currentEvent === 'complete') {
   103→              spinner.succeed('Analysis complete');
   104→              result = data;
   105→            } else if (currentEvent === 'error') {
   106→              spinner.fail((data.error as string) || 'Generation failed');
   107→            }
   108→          } catch {
   109→            // Skip malformed SSE events (e.g., truncated JSON from network issues)
   110→          }
   111→
   112→          currentEvent = '';
   113→          currentData = '';
   114→        }
   115→      }
   116→    }
   117→  } finally {
   118→    reader.releaseLock();
   119→  }
   120→
   121→  return result;
   122→}
   123→
   124→async function backfillBatch(
   125→  baseUrl: string,
   126→  sessionIds: string[],
   127→  offset: number,
   128→  total: number,
   129→  signal?: AbortSignal
   130→): Promise<{ completed: number; failed: number }> {
   131→  const res = await fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/facets/backfill`, {
   132→    method: 'POST',
   133→    headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
   134→    // force=true so outdated sessions (which already have facets) are re-processed.
   135→    // Missing sessions are unaffected — the guard only fires when a row exists.
   136→    body: JSON.stringify({ sessionIds, force: true }),
   137→    signal,
   138→  });
   139→
   140→  if (!res.ok) {
   141→    const text = await res.text().catch(() => res.statusText);
   142→    throw new Error(`Server error ${res.status}: ${text}`);
   143→  }
   144→  if (!res.body) throw new Error('No response body');
   145→
   146→  const reader = res.body.pipeThrough(new TextDecoderStream()).getReader();
   147→  let buffer = '';
   148→  let currentEvent = '';
   149→  let currentData = '';
   150→  let result = { completed: 0, failed: 0 };
   151→
   152→  const spinner = ora({ text: `  Backfilling ${offset + 1}-${offset + sessionIds.length} of ${total}...`, indent: 2 }).start();
   153→
   154→  try {
   155→    while (true) {
   156→      const { done, value } = await reader.read();
   157→      if (done) break;
   158→      buffer += value;
   159→      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
   160→      buffer = lines.pop() ?? '';
   161→      for (const line of lines) {
   162→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
   163→          currentEvent = line.slice(7).trim();
   164→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
   165→          currentData = line.slice(6);
   166→        } else if (line === '' && currentEvent && currentData) {
   167→          try {
   168→            const data = JSON.parse(currentData) as Record<string, unknown>;
   169→            if (currentEvent === 'progress') {
   170→              const processed = (data.completed as number) + (data.failed as number);
   171→              spinner.text = `  Backfilling ${offset + processed + 1} of ${total}...`;
   172→            } else if (currentEvent === 'complete') {
   173→              result = { completed: data.completed as number, failed: data.failed as number };
   174→              spinner.succeed(`  Batch complete: ${result.completed} extracted, ${result.failed} failed`);
   175→            }
   176→          } catch { /* skip malformed */ }
   177→          currentEvent = '';
   178→          currentData = '';
   179→        }
   180→      }
   181→    }
   182→  } finally {
   183→    reader.releaseLock();
   184→  }
   185→  return result;
   186→}
   187→
   188→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   189→// Actions
   190→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   191→
   192→// Mirrors formatIsoWeek/parseIsoWeek in server/src/routes/shared-aggregation.ts
   193→// -- kept here so the CLI doesn't import server code.
   194→// IMPORTANT: keep in sync with the canonical server implementation.
   195→function getCurrentIsoWeek(): string {
   196→  const now = new Date();
   197→  const nowDay = now.getUTCDay();
   198→  const daysToMonday = nowDay === 0 ? 6 : nowDay - 1;
   199→  const monday = new Date(now.getTime() - daysToMonday * 86400000);
   200→
   201→  const thursday = new Date(monday.getTime() + 3 * 86400000);
   202→  const year = thursday.getUTCFullYear();
   203→
   204→  const jan4 = new Date(Date.UTC(year, 0, 4));
   205→  const jan4Day = jan4.getUTCDay();
   206→  const daysToW1Monday = jan4Day === 0 ? 6 : jan4Day - 1;
   207→  const week1Monday = new Date(jan4.getTime() - daysToW1Monday * 86400000);
   208→
   209→  const weekNum = Math.round((monday.getTime() - week1Monday.getTime()) / (7 * 86400000)) + 1;
   210→  return `${year}-W${String(weekNum).padStart(2, '0')}`;
   211→}
   212→
   213→const ISO_WEEK_RE = /^(\d{4})-W(\d{2})$/;
   214→
   215→async function reflectAction(options: {
   216→  section?: string;
   217→  week?: string;
   218→  project?: string;
   219→}): Promise<void> {
   220→  const baseUrl = getBaseUrl();
   221→  const week = options.week || getCurrentIsoWeek();
   222→
   223→  // Validate --week format: must be YYYY-WNN with year 2020-2100 and week number 1-53
   224→  if (options.week) {
   225→    const match = ISO_WEEK_RE.exec(options.week);
   226→    const year = match ? parseInt(match[1], 10) : 0;
   227→    const weekNum = match ? parseInt(match[2], 10) : 0;
   228→    if (!match || weekNum < 1 || weekNum > 53 || year < 2020 || year > 2100) {
   229→      console.log(chalk.red('  Invalid week format: "' + options.week + '"'));
   230→      console.log(chalk.dim('  Use YYYY-WNN format with year 2020-2100, e.g., 2026-W10'));
   231→      console.log();
   232→      process.exit(1);
   233→    }
   234→  }
   235→
   236→  await checkServer(baseUrl);
   237→
   238→  console.log(chalk.dim(`  Generating reflection for ${week}...`));
   239→
   240→  // Check minimum session threshold
   241→  const checkParams = new URLSearchParams();
   242→  checkParams.set('period', week);
   243→  if (options.project) checkParams.set('project', options.project);
   244→  const aggRes = await fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/facets/aggregated?${checkParams.toString()}`);
   245→  if (aggRes.ok) {
   246→    const agg = await aggRes.json() as { totalSessions: number; totalAllSessions: number };
   247→    if (agg.totalSessions < 8) {
   248→      console.log(chalk.yellow(`  Not enough analyzed sessions for meaningful synthesis.`));
   249→      console.log(chalk.dim(`  Need at least 8 sessions with facets this week (currently ${agg.totalSessions}).`));
   250→      console.log(chalk.dim(`  Run session analysis to extract facets from more sessions.`));
   251→      console.log();
   252→      process.exit(1);
   253→    }
   254→
   255→    if (agg.totalAllSessions > 0 && agg.totalSessions / agg.totalAllSessions < 0.5) {
   256→      console.log(chalk.yellow(`  Note: Only ${agg.totalSessions} of ${agg.totalAllSessions} sessions are analyzed.`));
   257→      console.log(chalk.dim(`  Results may not represent your full patterns.`));
   258→      console.log();
   259→    }
   260→  }
   261→
   262→  const body: Record<string, unknown> = {
   263→    period: week,
   264→  };
   265→  if (options.section) {
   266→    body.sections = [options.section];
   267→  }
   268→  if (options.project) {
   269→    body.project = options.project;
   270→  }
   271→
   272→  console.log();
   273→  const data = await fetchWithSSE(`${baseUrl}/api/reflect/generate`, body);
   274→
   275→  // Display results summary
   276→  const results = data.results as Record<string, Record<string, unknown>> | undefined;
   277→  if (!results) {
   278→    console.log(chalk.dim('  No results generated.'));
   279→    return;
   280→  }
   281→
   282→  console.log();
   283→
   284→  // Friction & Wins summary
   285→  const frictionWins = results['friction-wins'];
   286→  if (frictionWins) {
   287→    console.log(chalk.bold('  Friction & Wins'));
   288→    if (frictionWins.narrative) {
   289→      const lines = String(frictionWins.narrative).split('\n');
   290→      for (const line of lines) {
   291→        console.log(chalk.dim('  ') + line);
   292→      }
   293→    }
   294→    console.log();
   295→  }
   296→
   297→  // Rules & Hooks summary
   298→  const rulesSkills = results['rules-skills'];
   299→  if (rulesSkills) {
   300→    console.log(chalk.bold('  Rules & Hooks'));
   301→    const rules = rulesSkills.claudeMdRules as Array<{ rule: string }> | undefined;
   302→    if (rules && rules.length > 0) {
   303→      console.log(chalk.dim('  CLAUDE.md rules:'));
   304→      for (const r of rules) {
   305→        console.log(`    ${chalk.cyan('→')} ${r.rule}`);
   306→      }
   307→    }
   308→    const hooks = rulesSkills.hookConfigs as Array<{ event: string; command: string }> | undefined;
   309→    if (hooks && hooks.length > 0) {
   310→      console.log(chalk.dim('  Hooks:'));
   311→      for (const h of hooks) {
   312→        console.log(`    ${chalk.cyan('→')} ${h.event}: ${h.command}`);
   313→      }
   314→    }
   315→    console.log();
   316→  }
   317→
   318→  // Working Style summary
   319→  const workingStyle = results['working-style'];
   320→  if (workingStyle) {
   321→    console.log(chalk.bold('  Working Style'));
   322→    if (workingStyle.narrative) {
   323→      const lines = String(workingStyle.narrative).split('\n');
   324→      for (const line of lines) {
   325→        console.log(chalk.dim('  ') + line);
   326→      }
   327→    }
   328→    console.log();
   329→  }
   330→
   331→  console.log(chalk.dim('  View full results: code-insights dashboard → Patterns'));
   332→  console.log();
   333→}
   334→
   335→const BACKFILL_BATCH_SIZE = 200;
   336→
   337→async function backfillAction(options: {
   338→  period?: string;
   339→  project?: string;
   340→  dryRun?: boolean;
   341→}): Promise<void> {
   342→  const baseUrl = getBaseUrl();
   343→  await checkServer(baseUrl);
   344→  await checkLlmConfigured(baseUrl);
   345→
   346→  const params = new URLSearchParams();
   347→  params.set('period', options.period || 'all');
   348→  if (options.project) params.set('project', options.project);
   349→
   350→  const missingRes = await fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/facets/missing?${params.toString()}`);
   351→  if (!missingRes.ok) {
   352→    const text = await missingRes.text().catch(() => missingRes.statusText);
   353→    console.log(chalk.red(`  Error: ${text}`));
   354→    process.exit(1);
   355→  }
   356→
   357→  const { sessionIds: missingIds, count: missingCount } = await missingRes.json() as { sessionIds: string[]; count: number };
   358→
   359→  const outdatedRes = await fetch(`${baseUrl}/api/facets/outdated?${params.toString()}`);
   360→  if (!outdatedRes.ok) {
   361→    const text = await outdatedRes.text().catch(() => outdatedRes.statusText);
   362→    console.log(chalk.red(`  Error fetching outdated sessions: ${text}`));
   363→    process.exit(1);
   364→  }
   365→
   366→  const { sessionIds: outdatedIds, count: outdatedCount } = await outdatedRes.json() as { sessionIds: string[]; count: number };
   367→
   368→  // Merge and deduplicate — a session could theoretically appear in both lists
   369→  // (e.g., if it got a partial facets row). Set deduplication ensures we count correctly.
   370→  const mergedSet = new Set([...missingIds, ...outdatedIds]);
   371→  const sessionIds = Array.from(mergedSet);
   372→  const count = sessionIds.length;
   373→
   374→  console.log();
   375→  if (count === 0) {
   376→    console.log(chalk.green('  All analyzed sessions already have up-to-date facets.'));
   377→    console.log();
   378→    return;
   379→  }
   380→
   381→  if (missingCount > 0 && outdatedCount > 0) {
   382→    console.log(chalk.cyan(`  Found ${missingCount} session${missingCount !== 1 ? 's' : ''} missing facets and ${outdatedCount} with outdated analysis. Processing ${count} total.`));
   383→  } else if (missingCount > 0) {
   384→    console.log(chalk.cyan(`  Found ${missingCount} session${missingCount !== 1 ? 's' : ''} with insights but missing facets.`));
   385→  } else {
   386→    console.log(chalk.cyan(`  Found ${outdatedCount} session${outdatedCount !== 1 ? 's' : ''} with outdated analysis.`));
   387→  }
   388→  console.log(chalk.dim(`  This will make ${count} LLM call${count !== 1 ? 's' : ''}.`));
   389→
   390→  if (options.dryRun) {
   391→    console.log(chalk.dim('  (dry run — no changes made)'));
   392→    console.log();
   393→    return;
   394→  }
   395→
   396→  // Confirm before proceeding — each call costs tokens
   397→  const confirmed = await confirmPrompt('  Continue?');
   398→  if (!confirmed) {
   399→    console.log(chalk.dim('  Aborted.'));
   400→    console.log();
   401→    return;
   402→  }
   403→
   404→  console.log();
   405→
   406→  let totalCompleted = 0;
   407→  let totalFailed = 0;
   408→
   409→  for (let i = 0; i < sessionIds.length; i += BACKFILL_BATCH_SIZE) {
   410→    const batch = sessionIds.slice(i, i + BACKFILL_BATCH_SIZE);
   411→    const { completed, failed } = await backfillBatch(baseUrl, batch, i, sessionIds.length);
   412→    totalCompleted += completed;
   413→    totalFailed += failed;
   414→  }
   415→
   416→  console.log();
   417→  console.log(chalk.bold('  Summary'));
   418→  console.log(chalk.green(`    ${totalCompleted} sessions backfilled`));
   419→  if (totalFailed > 0) {
   420→    console.log(chalk.yellow(`    ${totalFailed} sessions failed`));
   421→  }
   422→  console.log();
   423→}
   424→
   425→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   426→// Command registration
   427→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   428→
   429→const backfillCommand = new Command('backfill')
   430→  .description('Extract facets for sessions that have insights but are missing pattern data')
   431→  .option('-p, --period <period>', 'Time range: 7d, 30d, 90d, all', 'all')
   432→  .option('--project <name>', 'Scope to a single project')
   433→  .option('--dry-run', 'Show count without backfilling')
   434→  .action(backfillAction);
   435→
   436→export const reflectCommand = new Command('reflect')
   437→  .description('Generate cross-session analysis (friction, rules, working style)')
   438→  .option('--section <name>', 'Generate specific section: friction-wins, rules-skills, working-style')
   439→  .option('--week <week>', 'ISO week to reflect on (e.g., 2026-W10), defaults to current week')
   440→  .option('--project <name>', 'Scope to a single project')
   441→  .addCommand(backfillCommand)
   442→  .action(reflectAction);
   443→

     1→import { Hono } from 'hono';

     5→import { extractFacetsOnly } from '../llm/analysis.js';

     7→import { buildWhereClause, getAggregatedData } from './shared-aggregation.js';
     8→
     9→const app = new Hono();
    10→
    11→const MAX_BACKFILL_SESSIONS = 200;
    12→
    13→interface FacetRow {
    14→  session_id: string;
    15→  outcome_satisfaction: string;
    16→  workflow_pattern: string | null;
    17→  had_course_correction: number;
    18→  course_correction_reason: string | null;

    25→
    26→// GET /api/facets
    27→// Query params: project (project_id), period (7d|30d|90d|all), source (source_tool filter)
    28→// Returns: { facets, missingCount, totalSessions }
    29→app.get('/', (c) => {
    30→  const db = getDb();
    31→  const project = c.req.query('project');
    32→  const period = c.req.query('period') || '30d';
    33→  const source = c.req.query('source');
    34→
    35→  const { where, params } = buildWhereClause(period, project, source);
    36→
    37→  // Total sessions in scope
    38→  const totalRow = db.prepare(
    39→    `SELECT COUNT(*) as count FROM sessions s ${where}`
    40→  ).get(...params) as { count: number };
    41→
    42→  // Sessions with facets — join to sessions so period/project/source filters apply
    43→  const facets = db.prepare(
    44→    `SELECT sf.* FROM session_facets sf
    45→     JOIN sessions s ON sf.session_id = s.id
    46→     ${where}
    47→     ORDER BY s.started_at DESC`
    48→  ).all(...params) as FacetRow[];
    49→
    50→  return c.json({
    51→    facets,
    52→    missingCount: totalRow.count - facets.length,
    53→    totalSessions: totalRow.count,
    54→  });
    55→});
    56→
    57→// GET /api/facets/aggregated
    58→// Returns pre-aggregated friction categories and effective patterns for synthesis.
    59→// Uses the shared getAggregatedData function to avoid duplication with reflect routes.
    60→app.get('/aggregated', (c) => {
    61→  const db = getDb();
    62→  const project = c.req.query('project');
    63→  const period = c.req.query('period') || '30d';
    64→  const source = c.req.query('source');
    65→
    66→  const { where, params } = buildWhereClause(period, project, source);
    67→  const aggregated = getAggregatedData(db, where, params, project, source);
    68→
    69→  return c.json(aggregated);
    70→});
    71→
    72→// GET /api/facets/missing
    73→// Returns session IDs that have insights but no session_facets row.
    74→// Used by CLI `reflect backfill` and dashboard facet status indicators.
    75→app.get('/missing', (c) => {
    76→  const db = getDb();
    77→  const period = c.req.query('period') || 'all';
    78→  const project = c.req.query('project');
    79→  const source = c.req.query('source');
    80→
    81→  // buildWhereClause can't be used here — it generates "WHERE ..." prefix,
    82→  // but this query already needs "WHERE sf.session_id IS NULL".
    83→  // Build conditions inline instead.
    84→  const conditions: string[] = ['sf.session_id IS NULL', 's.deleted_at IS NULL'];
    85→  const params: (string | number)[] = [];
    86→
    87→  if (period !== 'all') {
    88→    const now = new Date();
    89→    const days = period === '7d' ? 7 : period === '30d' ? 30 : period === '90d' ? 90 : 0;
    90→    if (days > 0) {
    91→      conditions.push('s.started_at >= ?');
    92→      params.push(new Date(now.getTime() - days * 86400000).toISOString());
    93→    }
    94→  }
    95→  if (project) {
    96→    conditions.push('s.project_id = ?');
    97→    params.push(project);
    98→  }
    99→  if (source) {
   100→    conditions.push('s.source_tool = ?');
   101→    params.push(source);
   102→  }
   103→
   104→  const where = `WHERE ${conditions.join(' AND ')}`;
   105→
   106→  const rows = db.prepare(`
   107→    SELECT DISTINCT i.session_id
   108→    FROM insights i
   109→    JOIN sessions s ON i.session_id = s.id
   110→    LEFT JOIN session_facets sf ON i.session_id = sf.session_id
   111→    ${where}
   112→  `).all(...params) as Array<{ session_id: string }>;
   113→
   114→  const sessionIds = rows.map(r => r.session_id);
   115→  return c.json({ sessionIds, count: sessionIds.length });
   116→});
   117→
   118→// GET /api/facets/outdated
   119→// Returns count and sessionIds of session_facets rows where:
   120→//   - effective_patterns entries lack a category or driver field, OR
   121→//   - friction_points entries lack an attribution field
   122→// Accepts period + project to scope to the user's current view — avoids misleading counts
   123→// when the user is viewing "last 7 days" but sees outdated sessions from all time.
   124→app.get('/outdated', (c) => {
   125→  const db = getDb();
   126→  const project = c.req.query('project');
   127→  const period = c.req.query('period') || '30d';
   128→
   129→  const { where, params } = buildWhereClause(period, project);
   130→
   131→  // UNION of two subqueries — each finds session_ids with a specific outdated signal.
   132→  // UNION (not UNION ALL) deduplicates sessions that fail both checks.
   133→  // The effective_patterns arm uses OR to catch both missing category and missing driver
   134→  // in a single scan rather than two separate UNION arms.
   135→  const rows = db.prepare(`
   136→    SELECT DISTINCT sf.session_id
   137→    FROM session_facets sf
   138→    JOIN sessions s ON sf.session_id = s.id
   139→    CROSS JOIN json_each(sf.effective_patterns) je
   140→    ${where}
   141→    AND json_array_length(sf.effective_patterns) > 0
   142→    AND (json_extract(je.value, '$.category') IS NULL
   143→         OR json_extract(je.value, '$.driver') IS NULL)
   144→    UNION
   145→    SELECT DISTINCT sf.session_id
   146→    FROM session_facets sf
   147→    JOIN sessions s ON sf.session_id = s.id
   148→    CROSS JOIN json_each(sf.friction_points) je
   149→    ${where}
   150→    AND json_array_length(sf.friction_points) > 0
   151→    AND json_extract(je.value, '$.attribution') IS NULL
   152→  `).all(...params, ...params) as Array<{ session_id: string }>;
   153→
   154→  const sessionIds = rows.map(r => r.session_id);
   155→  return c.json({ count: sessionIds.length, sessionIds });
   156→});
   157→
   158→// POST /api/facets/backfill
   159→// Body: { sessionIds: string[], force?: boolean }
   160→// Streams progress as facets are extracted one-by-one for sessions that lack them.
   161→// force=true skips the existing-facets guard, allowing re-extraction of outdated rows.
   162→// Uses extractFacetsOnly (lightweight prompt: summary + first/last 20 messages).
   163→app.post('/backfill', async (c) => {
   164→  if (!isLLMConfigured()) {
   165→    return c.json({ error: 'LLM not configured.' }, 400);
   166→  }
   167→
   168→  const body = await c.req.json<{ sessionIds?: string[]; force?: boolean }>();
   169→  if (!body.sessionIds || !Array.isArray(body.sessionIds) || body.sessionIds.length === 0) {
   170→    return c.json({ error: 'sessionIds array required' }, 400);
   171→  }
   172→  if (body.sessionIds.length > MAX_BACKFILL_SESSIONS) {
   173→    return c.json({ error: `Maximum ${MAX_BACKFILL_SESSIONS} sessions per backfill request` }, 400);
   174→  }
   175→
   176→  const db = getDb();
   177→
   178→  return streamSSE(c, async (stream) => {
   179→    const abortSignal = c.req.raw.signal;
   180→    let completed = 0;
   181→    let failed = 0;
   182→    const total = body.sessionIds!.length;
   183→
   184→    for (const sessionId of body.sessionIds!) {
   185→      if (abortSignal.aborted) break;
   186→
   187→      const session = db.prepare(
   188→        `SELECT id, project_id, project_name, project_path, summary, ended_at
   189→         FROM sessions WHERE id = ? AND deleted_at IS NULL`
   190→      ).get(sessionId) as SessionData | undefined;
   191→
   192→      if (!session) {
   193→        failed++;
   194→        await stream.writeSSE({
   195→          event: 'progress',
   196→          data: JSON.stringify({
   197→            completed,
   198→            failed,
   199→            total,
   200→            currentSessionId: sessionId,
   201→          }),
   202→        });
   203→        continue;
   204→      }
   205→
   206→      // Skip sessions that already have facets unless force=true (used when re-processing
   207→      // outdated sessions that have stale attribution/driver/category fields).
   208→      if (!body.force) {
   209→        const existingFacet = db.prepare(
   210→          'SELECT 1 FROM session_facets WHERE session_id = ?'
   211→        ).get(sessionId);
   212→        if (existingFacet) {
   213→          completed++;
   214→          await stream.writeSSE({
   215→            event: 'progress',
   216→            data: JSON.stringify({
   217→              completed,
   218→              failed,
   219→              total,
   220→              currentSessionId: sessionId,
   221→            }),
   222→          });
   223→          continue;
   224→        }
   225→      }
   226→
   227→      // Only load first 20 and last 20 messages for facet extraction
   228→      const firstMessages = db.prepare(
   229→        `SELECT id, session_id, type, content, thinking, tool_calls, tool_results, usage, timestamp, parent_id
   230→         FROM messages WHERE session_id = ? ORDER BY timestamp ASC LIMIT 20`
   231→      ).all(sessionId) as SQLiteMessageRow[];
   232→
   233→      const lastMessages = db.prepare(
   234→        `SELECT id, session_id, type, content, thinking, tool_calls, tool_results, usage, timestamp, parent_id
   235→         FROM messages WHERE session_id = ? ORDER BY timestamp DESC LIMIT 20`
   236→      ).all(sessionId) as SQLiteMessageRow[];
   237→
   238→      // Merge and deduplicate (for sessions with <= 40 messages, some overlap)
   239→      const seenIds = new Set<string>();
   240→      const messages: SQLiteMessageRow[] = [];
   241→      for (const msg of [...firstMessages, ...lastMessages.reverse()]) {
   242→        if (!seenIds.has(msg.id)) {
   243→          seenIds.add(msg.id);
   244→          messages.push(msg);
   245→        }
   246→      }
   247→
   248→      const result = await extractFacetsOnly(session, messages, { signal: abortSignal });
   249→      if (result.success) {
   250→        completed++;
   251→      } else {
   252→        failed++;
   253→      }
   254→
   255→      await stream.writeSSE({
   256→        event: 'progress',
   257→        data: JSON.stringify({
   258→          completed,
   259→          failed,
   260→          total,
   261→          currentSessionId: sessionId,
   262→          ...(result.success ? {} : { error: result.error }),
   263→        }),
   264→      });
   265→    }
   266→
   267→    await stream.writeSSE({
   268→      event: 'complete',
   269→      data: JSON.stringify({ completed, failed, total }),
   270→    });
   271→  });
   272→});
   273→
   274→export default app;
   275→
```

   289→export async function analyzePromptQuality(
   290→  session: SessionData,
   291→  messages: SQLiteMessageRow[],
   292→  options?: AnalysisOptions
   293→): Promise<AnalysisResult> {
   294→  if (!isLLMConfigured()) {
   295→    return {
   296→      success: false,
   297→      insights: [],
   298→      error: 'LLM not configured. Run `code-insights config llm` to configure a provider.',
   299→    };
   300→  }
   301→
   302→  if (messages.length === 0) {
   303→    return {
   304→      success: false,
   305→      insights: [],
   306→      error: 'No messages found for this session.',
   307→    };
   308→  }
   309→
   310→  const userMessages = messages.filter(m => m.type === 'user');
   311→  if (userMessages.length < 2) {
   312→    return {
   313→      success: false,
   314→      insights: [],
   315→      error: 'Not enough user messages to analyze prompt quality (need at least 2).',
   316→    };
   317→  }
   318→
   319→  try {
   320→    const client = createLLMClient();
   321→    const formattedMessages = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages);
   322→
   323→    let analysisInput = formattedMessages;
   324→    const estimatedTokens = client.estimateTokens(formattedMessages);
   325→    if (estimatedTokens > MAX_INPUT_TOKENS) {
   326→      const targetLength = Math.floor((MAX_INPUT_TOKENS / estimatedTokens) * formattedMessages.length * 0.8);
   327→      analysisInput = formattedMessages.slice(0, targetLength) + '\n\n[... conversation truncated for analysis ...]';
   328→    }
   329→
   330→    const prompt = generatePromptQualityPrompt(
   331→      session.project_name,
   332→      analysisInput,
   333→      messages.length
   334→    );
   335→
   336→    options?.onProgress?.({ phase: 'analyzing' });
   337→    const response = await client.chat([
   338→      { role: 'system', content: PROMPT_QUALITY_SYSTEM_PROMPT },
   339→      { role: 'user', content: prompt },
   340→    ], { signal: options?.signal });
   341→
   342→    const parsed = parsePromptQualityResponse(response.content);
   343→    if (!parsed.success) {
   344→      return {
   345→        success: false,
   346→        insights: [],
   347→        error: 'Failed to parse prompt quality analysis. Please try again.',
   348→        error_type: parsed.error.error_type,
   349→        response_length: parsed.error.response_length,
   350→        response_preview: parsed.error.response_preview,
   351→      };
   352→    }
   353→
   354→    options?.onProgress?.({ phase: 'saving' });
   355→    const insight = convertPromptQualityToInsightRow(parsed.data, session);
   356→
   357→    // Save new insight, then delete old prompt_quality insights
   358→    saveInsightsToDb([insight]);
   359→    deleteSessionInsights(session.id, {
   360→      includeOnlyTypes: ['prompt_quality'],
   361→      excludeIds: [insight.id],
   362→    });
   363→
   364→    return {
   365→      success: true,
   366→      insights: [insight],
   367→      usage: response.usage ? {
   368→        inputTokens: response.usage.inputTokens,
   369→        outputTokens: response.usage.outputTokens,
   370→      } : undefined,
   371→    };
   372→  } catch (error) {
   373→    if (error instanceof Error && error.name === 'AbortError') {
   374→      return { success: false, insights: [], error: 'Analysis cancelled', error_type: 'abort' };
   375→    }
   376→    return {
   377→      success: false,
   378→      insights: [],
```

> AGENT

Good. I now have a complete picture. Here's my plan:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Current architecture separates facets and PQ into two isolated pipelines:**
- `POST /api/facets/backfill` → calls `extractFacetsOnly()` (lightweight prompt, summary + 20+20 messages)
- `POST /api/analysis/prompt-quality` → calls `analyzePromptQuality()` (full messages, user messages ≥ 2 required)

The backfill endpoint loops over sessions with SSE progress streaming. PQ analysis is currently only triggered per-session from the dashboard UI. There's no bulk PQ endpoint today.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Plan: Add `--prompt-quality` flag to `reflect backfill`

### Changes needed:

**1. New server endpoint: `POST /api/facets/backfill-pq`** (in `server/src/routes/facets.ts`)
- Accepts `{ sessionIds: string[], force?: boolean }`
- Streams SSE progress (same pattern as facets backfill)
- For each session: loads all messages, calls `analyzePromptQuality()`, replaces existing PQ insight
- `force=true` means overwrite even if PQ insight exists

**2. New server endpoint: `GET /api/facets/missing-pq`** (in `server/src/routes/facets.ts`)
- Returns session IDs that have insights (type != 'prompt_quality') but NO `prompt_quality` insight
- OR sessions where PQ insight exists but is outdated (missing new-schema fields like `metadata.findings`)

**3. CLI `reflect backfill` updates** (in `cli/src/commands/reflect.ts`)
- Add `--prompt-quality` flag
- When set: fetch missing/outdated PQ sessions, batch through `backfill-pq` endpoint
- Messaging: "Found X sessions needing prompt quality re-analysis. This will make X LLM calls."
- When both flags apply (no flag = facets only, `--prompt-quality` = PQ only), keep them separate phases

**4. Dashboard UI indicator** (deferred — you mentioned showing a flag for sessions with PQ but no insights)
- Sessions with PQ but no insights already can't be PQ-backfilled via CLI (they need full analysis first)
- The dashboard already has an "Analyze" button per session — this covers it

### Files to modify:
| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `server/src/routes/facets.ts` | Add `GET /missing-pq` + `POST /backfill-pq` endpoints |
| `server/src/llm/analysis.ts` | Export `analyzePromptQuality` (already exported) — no changes |
| `cli/src/commands/reflect.ts` | Add `--prompt-quality` flag, new `backfillPqBatch()` helper, updated `backfillAction()` |

### What this does NOT change:
- No schema changes needed — PQ data lives in `insights` table as `type='prompt_quality'`
- No changes to the PQ analysis function itself
- No changes to reflect prompts (the second question about PQ feeding into reflect — we'll address that separately with TA + LLM Expert after getting fresh PQ data)

Want me to proceed with this plan on a feature branch?

# Architecture Review: Where Should Friction Points and Effective Patterns Come From?

## 1. What Each Pipeline Actually Extracts

### Session Insights/Facets Pipeline (SESSION_ANALYSIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT)

**Input:** The full conversation transcript (all messages — user, assistant, system, tool calls, tool results, thinking).

**Outputs:**
- **Facets** (holistic session assessment):
  - `outcome_satisfaction` (high/medium/low/abandoned)
  - `workflow_pattern` (plan-then-implement, iterative-refinement, etc.)
  - `friction_points` (max 5): `_reasoning`, `category`, `attribution`, `description`, `severity`, `resolution`
  - `effective_patterns` (max 3): `_reasoning`, `category`, `description`, `confidence`, `driver`
  - `had_course_correction`, `course_correction_reason`, `iteration_count`
- **Insights:**
  - `summary` (title, content, outcome, bullets)
  - `decisions` (max 3): situation, choice, reasoning, alternatives, trade-offs, revisit_when
  - `learnings` (max 5): symptom, root_cause, takeaway, applies_when
  - `session_character` (deep_focus, bug_hunt, etc.)

**What the LLM sees:** Everything — the full conversation including AI reasoning, tool calls, tool results, and AI thinking blocks. It can observe the AI's behavior, mistakes, investigations, and course corrections.

### Prompt Quality Pipeline (PROMPT_QUALITY_SYSTEM_PROMPT)

**Input:** The same full conversation transcript (formatted identically).

**Outputs:**
- `efficiency_score` (0-100)
- `message_overhead` (how many messages could have been saved)
- `assessment` (2-3 sentence summary)
- `takeaways` (max 4): before/after prompt rewrites or reinforcement of good practices
- `findings` (max 8): `category`, `type` (deficit/strength), `description`, `message_ref`, `impact`, `confidence`, `suggested_improvement`
- `dimension_scores`: 5 numeric scores (context_provision, request_specificity, scope_management, information_timing, correction_quality)

**What the LLM sees:** The same conversation, but its instructions explicitly say "Focus on USER messages only — don't critique the assistant's responses."

## 2. Category Comparison — Are They Measuring the Same Thing?

### Friction Categories (9)
| Category | What it measures |
|----------|-----------------|
| `wrong-approach` | Strategy/architecture/tool mismatch for the task |
| `knowledge-gap` | Incorrect knowledge applied about a library/API/framework |
| `stale-assumptions` | Work proceeded from outdated assumptions about state |
| `incomplete-requirements` | Instructions missing critical context/constraints |
| `context-loss` | Prior decisions forgotten mid-session |
| `scope-creep` | Work expanded beyond stated task boundaries |
| `repeated-mistakes` | Same error recurred despite earlier correction |
| `documentation-gap` | Relevant docs existed but were inaccessible |
| `tooling-limitation` | Tool genuinely lacked a needed capability |

### PQ Deficit Categories (7)
| Category | What it measures |
|----------|-----------------|
| `vague-request` | Request lacked specificity for AI to act without guessing |
| `missing-context` | Critical background not provided by user |
| `late-constraint` | Requirement provided after AI already started different approach |
| `unclear-correction` | User said "wrong" without explaining what/why |
| `scope-drift` | Session objective shifted mid-conversation |
| `missing-acceptance-criteria` | User didn't define what success looks like |
| `assumption-not-surfaced` | User held unstated assumption AI couldn't infer |

### Overlap Analysis

There IS genuine overlap, but it is partial and asymmetric:

| Friction Category | Overlapping PQ Category | Same thing? |
|---|---|---|
| `incomplete-requirements` | `missing-context` + `vague-request` + `missing-acceptance-criteria` | **Partial.** Friction captures the GAP (requirements were missing). PQ captures WHO created the gap (user didn't provide them). But `incomplete-requirements` can also be attributed `ai-capability` or `environmental` — the friction version has an `attribution` field that disambiguates. |
| `scope-creep` | `scope-drift` | **Very close.** Both describe expanding scope. Friction tracks it as a session dynamics event with severity and resolution. PQ tracks it as a prompting behavior with a message_ref. |
| `context-loss` | `late-constraint` | **Different.** Context-loss = things established EARLIER were forgotten. Late-constraint = things the user KNEW but didn't share upfront. Opposite directions. |
| `wrong-approach` | (no equivalent) | **No overlap.** Wrong-approach is about strategy selection, which is primarily an AI or collaborative activity. PQ doesn't measure this at all. |
| `knowledge-gap` | (no equivalent) | **No overlap.** This is about applying incorrect technical knowledge. PQ doesn't assess AI knowledge. |
| `stale-assumptions` | (no equivalent) | **No overlap.** This is about working from outdated state. Not a prompting issue. |
| `repeated-mistakes` | (no equivalent) | **No overlap.** Session dynamics — the same mistake recurring. Not about user prompts. |
| `documentation-gap` | (no equivalent) | **No overlap.** Environmental issue. |
| `tooling-limitation` | (no equivalent) | **No overlap.** Environmental issue. |

**The overlap covers approximately 3 of 9 friction categories.** The remaining 6 cannot be derived from prompt quality analysis because they are not about user prompting behavior.

## 3. Assessment of the User's Hypothesis

**Hypothesis: "Friction points should come from PQ analysis, not session insights."**

**This hypothesis is incorrect.** Here is why:

### A. Different observation scopes

The session insights prompt has a critical capability the PQ prompt explicitly disclaims: it examines AI behavior. The system prompt says "You've just observed an AI-assisted coding session" and it receives AI thinking blocks, tool call sequences, and tool results. It can observe:

- The AI tried approach A, hit an error, then tried approach B (`wrong-approach`)
- The AI applied incorrect knowledge about an API (`knowledge-gap`)
- The AI forgot a constraint established 30 messages ago (`context-loss`)
- The same type checking error appeared 3 times (`repeated-mistakes`)
- The AI couldn't find documentation that existed (`documentation-gap`)

The PQ prompt is explicitly told "Focus on USER messages only — don't critique the assistant's responses." It cannot and should not identify friction caused by AI behavior.

### B. Attribution makes friction categories multi-actor

Each friction point carries an `attribution` field: `user-actionable`, `ai-capability`, or `environmental`. This is the design's key insight — friction is not exclusively about user prompting. The attribution decision tree (prompts.ts lines 103-107) explicitly handles:

- Step 1: External cause? -> `environmental`
- Step 2: User could have prevented? -> `user-actionable`
- Step 3: User was clear, AI still failed -> `ai-capability`

If friction only came from PQ analysis, you would lose the `ai-capability` and `environmental` attributed friction points entirely. These represent roughly half the friction taxonomy (5 of 9 categories are frequently non-user-actionable).

### C. PQ is a USER COACHING tool, friction is a SESSION DIAGNOSTIC tool

The PQ prompt's role declaration says: "You are a prompt engineering coach helping developers communicate more effectively with AI coding assistants." It produces actionable before/after prompt rewrites. It is prescriptive.

The session insights prompt's role declaration says: "You are a senior staff engineer writing entries for a team's engineering knowledge base." It produces factual assessments of what happened. It is descriptive.

These serve different product functions:
- **Friction** feeds Reflect's cross-session pattern analysis and eventually Progress tracking. It answers "what keeps going wrong in my sessions?" across all actors.
- **PQ** feeds user coaching and prompt improvement. It answers "how can I prompt better?"

### D. What the user might actually be sensing

The user's intuition likely stems from a real observation: some friction points (those attributed `user-actionable`) DO describe the same events as PQ deficit findings, just from different angles. Specifically:

- A `user-actionable` `incomplete-requirements` friction point and a `missing-context` PQ deficit finding might describe the exact same moment in the conversation.
- A `user-actionable` `scope-creep` friction point and a `scope-drift` PQ finding might describe the same event.

This is **genuine redundancy for the `user-actionable` subset of friction**, and the user is right to notice it. But the solution is not to move friction into PQ — it is to acknowledge they are complementary lenses on the same events.

## 4. What Would Actually Improve the System

### Option A: Do nothing (current approach)
**Verdict: Defensible but suboptimal.**

The current architecture correctly keeps friction and PQ separate. They serve different product layers (Reflect/Progress vs. coaching). The overlap is real but tolerable because they have different audiences within the product.

**Risk:** Users see a `user-actionable` `incomplete-requirements` friction point and a `missing-context` PQ deficit finding for the same session moment, and wonder why two different pipelines told them the same thing in different words.

### Option B: Cross-reference PQ findings into Reflect (enrichment)
**Verdict: Best improvement for the cost.**

The PQ deficit findings (`vague-request`, `missing-context`, `late-constraint`, etc.) are never consumed by Reflect today. The `shared-aggregation.ts` aggregation code has zero references to `prompt_quality`, `findings`, or `pq_`. This is a missed opportunity.

PQ deficit findings could enrich the Reflect narrative by providing a second signal for user-actionable friction. Concretely:
- When Reflect generates the Friction & Wins narrative, it could receive PQ deficit category counts alongside friction category counts.
- The LLM synthesizer could correlate: "Your most common friction is `incomplete-requirements` (7 occurrences), and PQ analysis shows `missing-context` as your most common deficit (12 occurrences across 8 sessions) — these are two views of the same gap."

This would be a small change: add PQ finding aggregation to `getAggregatedData()` and include it in the `generateFrictionWinsPrompt()` data payload.

**Token cost:** Minimal — PQ aggregates are pre-computed counts, not raw findings.

### Option C: Move friction into PQ (user's suggestion)
**Verdict: Wrong direction.**

This would lose:
1. `ai-capability` attributed friction (5 of 9 categories frequently use this)
2. `environmental` attributed friction (2 categories primarily use this)
3. Friction's severity and resolution tracking
4. The session-holistic perspective (PQ only looks at user messages)
5. Friction data for the Progress feature (which filters on `user-actionable` but needs the full picture for comparison)

### Option D: Derive user-actionable friction FROM PQ findings
**Verdict: Interesting but premature.**

You could theoretically stop the session insights prompt from emitting `user-actionable` friction and instead derive those from PQ deficit findings. This would eliminate the redundancy for that subset. But:
- It requires PQ to always run (currently it needs 2+ user messages)
- It breaks the single-LLM-call session analysis model
- It creates a dependency: friction data is incomplete until PQ runs
- The attribution decision tree in the session prompt currently handles this well enough

## 5. Recommendation

**The current architecture is fundamentally sound.** Friction and effective patterns belong in the session insights pipeline because that pipeline sees the full session (all actors), while PQ intentionally restricts itself to user behavior only.

**The actionable improvement is Option B:** feed PQ aggregates into Reflect as a complementary signal. This would cost approximately 20 lines of code in `shared-aggregation.ts` (add a PQ finding aggregation query) and 10 lines in `reflect-prompts.ts` (add PQ data to the Friction & Wins prompt payload). Zero prompt restructuring required.

The user-actionable friction / PQ deficit overlap is real but is a feature, not a bug — it provides two independent LLM assessments of the same events, which increases confidence in the classification. If deduplication becomes a UX issue in the dashboard, that is a presentation problem, not an architecture problem.

## Honest Reassessment: Should Friction/Patterns Move from Session Insights to PQ?

### Q1: Does the current session insights prompt try to do too much?

**Yes, clearly.** Reading the actual prompt text makes this undeniable.

The `SESSION_ANALYSIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (lines 259-367) has two completely different analytical modes crammed into one system prompt:

**Mode A — Knowledge Capture (lines 301-367, "PART 2 — INSIGHTS"):**
The system prompt persona is "a senior staff engineer writing entries for a team's engineering knowledge base." The audience is "a developer who has never seen this session but works on the same codebase." The outputs are summary, decisions, learnings, session_character. This is about **what was accomplished and what was learned** — knowledge artifacts for future reference.

**Mode B — Session Dynamics Analysis (lines 263-299, "PART 1 — SESSION FACETS"):**
This asks for outcome_satisfaction, workflow_pattern, friction_points (with 3-step attribution decision trees, CoT `_reasoning` scratchpads, contrastive category guidance), effective_patterns (with 4-step driver decision trees, baseline exclusion rules, contrastive pairs), course corrections, iteration counts. This is about **how the interaction went** — behavioral diagnostics.

The prompt literally says "PART 1" and "PART 2" — the author already recognized these are separate tasks. The system persona ("senior staff engineer writing knowledge base entries") is appropriate for Mode A but is a poor fit for Mode B. Classifying friction attribution using a 3-step decision tree and writing CoT reasoning chains is not what a knowledge-base author does.

**Token budget evidence:** The `FRICTION_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` constant is ~450 tokens. `EFFECTIVE_PATTERN_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` is ~500 tokens. These two guidance blocks alone consume ~950 tokens of the system prompt, and they exist purely for Mode B. The Mode A guidance (quality standards, examples, output schema) is another ~600 tokens. The system prompt is trying to make the LLM simultaneously be a knowledge-base author AND a behavioral diagnostician, and the guidance for each task is competing for the LLM's attention within a single generation.

**Verdict: The session insights prompt is objectively doing two different jobs.** This is not a judgment call — the prompt structure itself reveals it. "PART 1" and "PART 2" are different cognitive tasks with different analytical lenses.

---

### Q2: Would friction/patterns be BETTER served by the PQ prompt?

This is where the user's design intent is actually more coherent than the current implementation. Let me trace the analytical lenses:

**What PQ currently analyzes (lines 694-728):**
- "Identify each time the assistant asked for clarification that could have been avoided" — this IS friction analysis (specifically, `incomplete-requirements`, `vague-request`)
- "Each time the user corrected the assistant's interpretation" — this IS course correction / iteration analysis
- "Whether critical context or requirements were provided late" — this IS `late-constraint` friction
- "Moments where the user's prompt was notably well-crafted" — this IS effective pattern recognition (from the user side)

The PQ prompt already walks through the exact same conversational moments that produce friction_points and effective_patterns. It just captures them through a narrower lens (user prompting quality only) and throws away the AI-side and environmental observations.

**Overlap is already real:**
- PQ's `late-constraint` finding maps directly to friction's `incomplete-requirements` or `stale-assumptions`
- PQ's `scope-drift` finding maps directly to friction's `scope-creep` (this cross-taxonomy issue is already documented in memory)
- PQ's `missing-context` finding overlaps with friction's `knowledge-gap`
- PQ's `productive-correction` strength overlaps with facets' `had_course_correction`
- PQ's `message_overhead` metric overlaps with facets' `iteration_count`

Currently the LLM is asked to analyze the same conversational moments twice — once in session insights (as friction/patterns) and once in PQ (as deficits/strengths). The two analyses can disagree. A moment where the user provided a vague request might be classified as `incomplete-requirements` friction with `user-actionable` attribution in one call, and as a `vague-request` deficit in the other call. Or it might not — the LLM might notice different things in each pass because the prompts prime different analytical lenses.

**Would a combined "session dynamics" prompt produce better friction/pattern classifications?**

I think yes, for one specific reason: **friction attribution and prompt quality assessment are the same analytical judgment from different angles.** When the session insights prompt classifies friction as `user-actionable`, it is making a prompt quality judgment. When PQ classifies a moment as a `vague-request` deficit, it is identifying user-actionable friction. They are literally the same conclusion expressed in different taxonomies. Having one LLM call make both judgments simultaneously would produce more internally consistent results than two separate calls that might disagree.

---

### Q3: What about `ai-capability` and `environmental` attributed friction?

The previous analysis's objection ("PQ only looks at user messages") was correct about the current prompt instruction but wrong as an architectural constraint.

The PQ system prompt says: "Focus on USER messages only — don't critique the assistant's responses." This is a 12-word instruction (line 715). It exists because PQ was designed as a user-coaching tool. But:

1. **Nothing in the data pipeline prevents analyzing assistant messages.** Both `analyzeSession()` and `analyzePromptQuality()` receive the identical `messages: SQLiteMessageRow[]` array (lines 101 and 292). Both format with `formatMessagesForAnalysis()`. Both get the full conversation.

2. **The PQ dimension scores already implicitly assess AI behavior.** `correction_quality` asks "how well did the user redirect the AI" — this requires understanding WHAT the AI did wrong. You cannot score correction quality without analyzing the AI's mistake.

3. **If PQ became "session dynamics analysis," the scope instruction changes to:** "Analyze the full user-AI interaction. For prompting quality, focus on user contributions. For friction and patterns, assess all actors using the attribution/driver decision trees." This is ~30 words of prompt rewrite.

The real question is cognitive load. Would asking one LLM call to simultaneously:
- Score 5 PQ dimensions (user-focused)
- Classify up to 4 PQ takeaways with before/after examples
- Classify up to 8 PQ findings
- Classify up to 5 friction points with CoT attribution
- Classify up to 3 effective patterns with CoT driver reasoning

...exceed the LLM's ability to do all well?

**Honest answer: it depends on the model.** For Sonnet 3.5/4 or GPT-4o, this is within capability — the total output is ~2,500-3,000 tokens, well within comfort range. For smaller Ollama models (Llama 3 8B), this would likely degrade quality. The current split into two calls gives each call a simpler cognitive task, which helps weaker models.

---

### Q4: What would a merged approach look like?

**Option A: Full merge (PQ absorbs friction/patterns)**

Session Insights becomes:
- System prompt: "senior staff engineer, knowledge base author"
- Output: `summary`, `decisions`, `learnings`, `session_character`
- Token budget: ~1,200 input (system + guidance), ~1,000-1,500 output
- Character: pure knowledge capture, clean single-purpose prompt

Session Dynamics (renamed PQ) becomes:
- System prompt: "session dynamics analyst and prompt coach"
- Output: `efficiency_score`, `assessment`, `takeaways`, `findings`, `dimension_scores`, `friction_points`, `effective_patterns`, `outcome_satisfaction`, `workflow_pattern`, `iteration_count`, `had_course_correction`
- Token budget: ~2,800 input (system + friction guidance + pattern guidance + PQ guidance), ~2,000-2,500 output
- Character: behavioral analysis, all actors, coaching + diagnostics

**Token impact:**
- Current: Call 1 (session insights) ~2,200 input + Call 2 (PQ) ~2,250 input = ~4,450 total input tokens for guidance
- Merged: Call 1 (knowledge capture) ~1,200 input + Call 2 (dynamics) ~2,800 input = ~4,000 total input tokens
- Net savings: ~450 input tokens (the friction/pattern guidance no longer duplicated in FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT context)
- Output tokens: roughly the same total, just redistributed

**Quality prediction:**
- Knowledge capture call: BETTER. Freed from Mode B, it can focus entirely on extracting decisions/learnings. The LLM's attention isn't split between writing knowledge-base entries and doing behavioral classification.
- Session dynamics call: COMPARABLE to SLIGHTLY BETTER. The LLM already does friction analysis and PQ analysis over the same conversation — doing them in one pass lets it maintain a single mental model of the session dynamics instead of building it twice.

**Option B: Keep the split but rationalize it (deduplicate the overlap)**

Don't merge, but explicitly assign:
- Session insights: summary, decisions, learnings, session_character, outcome_satisfaction, workflow_pattern (these are knowledge-capture-adjacent)
- PQ: prompting quality scores, takeaways, findings (user-focused coaching)
- Friction/patterns stay in session insights

This preserves the current architecture but doesn't address the user's design intent or the dual-mode problem in the session insights prompt.

**Option C: Three calls (the "right" answer if cost didn't matter)**

1. Knowledge capture: summary, decisions, learnings
2. Session dynamics: friction, patterns, outcome, workflow, course corrections
3. Prompt coaching: PQ scores, takeaways, findings

Each call has a clean single purpose. But 3 LLM calls per session is expensive, and calls 2 and 3 have enough overlap that merging them (Option A) is more practical.

---

### Q5: Practical considerations

**Backfill impact — the real obstacle:**

Currently, `extractFacetsOnly()` (lines 882-937 in analysis.ts) uses a lightweight prompt with only summary + first/last 20 messages. This is how backfill works for sessions that already have insights but lack facets. It costs ~2,500 input tokens instead of sending the full conversation.

If friction/patterns move to the dynamics prompt, backfill would need the full transcript to produce them alongside PQ analysis. The lightweight prompt can't do PQ assessment (it needs all user messages, not just first/last 20).

**However:** this is a transitional concern, not a permanent one. Backfill exists for legacy sessions. Going forward, new sessions would always get both calls with full transcripts. The question is whether the ~$0.01-0.03 extra cost per backfilled session matters. For a local-first tool with typically 50-200 sessions to backfill, this is $0.50-6.00 total — negligible.

**The more practical question:** Can backfill be decoupled? Could you run a "facet-only backfill" that just does friction/patterns from the lightweight prompt (accepting lower quality for historical data), and only use the full dynamics prompt going forward? Yes — the current `FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` already does this. It would just become the "legacy backfill" path, not the primary path.

**Data pipeline:**

Currently:
- `analyzeSession()` saves insights to `insights` table AND facets to `session_facets` table
- `analyzePromptQuality()` saves to `insights` table only (type: `prompt_quality`)

In the merged approach:
- Knowledge capture call saves insights (summary, decision, learning) + session_character
- Dynamics call saves PQ insight (type: `prompt_quality`) + facets to `session_facets`

The `deleteSessionInsights` logic (lines 248-254) already handles type-based exclusion. The `saveFacetsToDb` function is independent of which call produced the data. The pipeline restructuring is mechanical, not architectural.

**Chunking:**

For sessions exceeding `MAX_INPUT_TOKENS`, the current code chunks messages for session insights (line 129) and truncates for PQ (line 326). In the merged approach:
- Knowledge capture: still chunks (summary/decisions/learnings can merge across chunks)
- Dynamics: truncate (PQ already does this; friction/patterns are holistic assessments that don't merge well across chunks)
- For chunked sessions, fall back to `FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` for friction/patterns (same as current behavior)

---

### Balanced Assessment

| Criterion | Current (friction in session insights) | Proposed (friction in dynamics/PQ) |
|-----------|---------------------------------------|-------------------------------------|
| Separation of concerns | **Worse.** Session insights prompt is explicitly dual-mode ("PART 1" / "PART 2"). | **Better.** Knowledge capture vs. behavioral analysis is a clean split. |
| Friction/pattern quality | **Baseline.** Works, but shares LLM attention with knowledge extraction. | **Comparable to slightly better.** Friction/patterns analyzed alongside PQ findings, which are the same conversational moments viewed from a different angle. Internal consistency improves. |
| PQ quality | **Baseline.** Clean single-purpose prompt today. | **Risk of slight degradation.** Adding friction/patterns increases cognitive load on the PQ call. Mitigated by the fact that the analysis is closely related. |
| Token efficiency | ~4,450 input tokens total | ~4,000 input tokens total (friction/pattern guidance no longer in both session + facet-only prompts) |
| Backfill | Lightweight prompt works today | Requires full transcript or acceptance of lower-quality legacy backfill |
| Implementation effort | Status quo — zero effort | Medium — prompt restructuring, analysis.ts pipeline changes, facet save location changes |
| Model compatibility | Two simpler calls = better for weak models | One heavier call = riskier for small Ollama models |
| Conceptual coherence | **Weaker.** "Knowledge base author" persona doing behavioral classification. | **Stronger.** "Session dynamics analyst" persona doing behavioral classification. |

### My Honest Conclusion

The user's design intent is more coherent than the current implementation. The session insights prompt is objectively doing two jobs, and the friction/pattern classification task is more naturally aligned with PQ's analytical lens (examining interaction dynamics) than with the knowledge-capture lens (extracting decisions and learnings for a knowledge base).

The strongest argument FOR the current arrangement is practical inertia and model compatibility with smaller LLMs. The strongest argument FOR the proposed arrangement is conceptual cleanliness and the elimination of redundant analysis (the LLM currently examines the same conversational moments twice through different lenses that partially overlap).

If I were designing this from scratch, I would build it the way the user describes: knowledge capture in one call, session dynamics (including PQ + friction + patterns) in another. The fact that the current session insights prompt already uses "PART 1" / "PART 2" labeling is a code smell — it acknowledges the two tasks are distinct but keeps them coupled anyway.

**The third option I haven't discussed:** You could also consider making friction/patterns a SHARED output produced by the dynamics call but STORED alongside session facets (as they are today). This means the dynamics call produces PQ insight + facets, while session insights produces knowledge-capture insights only. The database schema doesn't change. The `session_facets` table stays where it is. Only the source of the data changes. This is the lowest-risk migration path and addresses the core issue (session insights prompt doing two jobs) without touching the storage layer.

## The LLM Expert Agrees With Your Design Intent

**The core finding:** The session insights prompt is objectively doing two jobs. It literally labels them "PART 1" (session dynamics) and "PART 2" (knowledge capture) — a code smell the Expert calls out directly.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The persona mismatch is revealing:** The session insights system prompt says *"You are a senior staff engineer writing entries for a team's engineering knowledge base."* That persona is perfect for extracting decisions, learnings, and summaries. But it's the wrong persona for classifying friction attribution using a 3-step decision tree with CoT reasoning chains. A "knowledge-base author" doesn't do behavioral diagnostics — a "session dynamics analyst" does.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## What the Evidence Shows

| Finding | Detail |
|---------|--------|
| **Prompt is dual-mode** | ~950 tokens of friction/pattern classification guidance competes for LLM attention with ~600 tokens of knowledge-capture guidance in one call |
| **PQ already analyzes friction moments** | PQ's `late-constraint`, `missing-context`, `scope-drift` findings walk through the exact same conversational moments that produce friction points — the LLM examines them twice |
| **No data access difference** | Both calls receive the identical `messages: SQLiteMessageRow[]` array. The "PQ only sees user messages" limitation is a 12-word prompt instruction, not a pipeline constraint |
| **Token impact is positive** | Current: ~4,450 input tokens across 2 calls. Proposed: ~4,000 (guidance no longer duplicated) |

## The Proposed Clean Split

| Call | Persona | Outputs | Purpose |
|------|---------|---------|---------|
| **Knowledge Capture** (session insights) | Senior staff engineer, knowledge-base author | summary, decisions, learnings, session_character | What was accomplished? What did we learn? (→ PR notes, Jira comments) |
| **Session Dynamics** (evolved PQ) | Session dynamics analyst + prompt coach | PQ scores, takeaways, findings, friction_points, effective_patterns, outcome_satisfaction, workflow_pattern, iteration_count, course_correction | How did the interaction go? How can I improve? (→ Reflect, Progress) |

## Honest Trade-offs

**In favor of this change:**
- Conceptually cleaner — each call has one job, one persona
- Friction/patterns analyzed alongside PQ findings (same conversational moments, one pass = more internally consistent)
- Eliminates redundant analysis of the same events through different lenses

**Risks to consider:**
- The dynamics call becomes heavier (~2,500 output tokens) — fine for Sonnet/GPT-4o, potentially risky for small Ollama models
- Backfill changes: the lightweight `extractFacetsOnly()` (summary + 20+20 messages) can't do PQ. Could keep it as a "legacy backfill" path
- Medium implementation effort — prompt restructuring + pipeline changes

**Lowest-risk migration path the Expert suggests:** Make the dynamics call produce PQ insight + facets. Store facets in `session_facets` exactly as today. Only the *source* of the data changes (from session insights call → dynamics call). Database schema stays identical.
