I now have all the information I need. Here is my structured review.

---

## TA Review: 3-Prompt Architecture for Session Analysis

### 1. Parallel Execution Feasibility

**Verdict: VALIDATED -- with one caveat**

There is no shared mutable state in the analysis functions. Each of `analyzeSession()`, `extractFacetsOnly()`, and `analyzePromptQuality()`:
- Creates its own `LLMClient` via `createLLMClient()` (stateless factory, reads config from disk each time -- line 35 of `client.ts`)
- Reads messages from SQLite (read-only, WAL mode handles concurrent reads)
- Writes to different tables/rows at the end (insights table for insights + PQ, session_facets table for facets, sessions table for character update)

**The caveat is SQLite write contention.** All three functions call `getDb()` and write when they finish. With better-sqlite3's synchronous API and WAL mode, concurrent writes from the same process are serialized at the JS event loop level (single-threaded), so there is no actual lock contention -- they just interleave. This is safe. If these were in separate processes, WAL write locks could cause SQLITE_BUSY, but since all three run as `Promise.all()` within the same Node.js process, this is a non-issue.

**No race conditions on the write side either.** Session insights write to the `insights` table with `INSERT OR REPLACE` keyed by UUID. Facets write to `session_facets` keyed by `session_id`. PQ writes a separate insight row. None of these conflict.

### 2. Chunking Simplification

**Verdict: VALIDATED -- chunking CAN be removed from `analyzeSession()`, but with a nuance**

Current chunking logic (lines 129-211 of `analysis.ts`) exists because the combined insights+facets prompt might exceed `MAX_INPUT_TOKENS` (80k). The chunking does two things:
1. Splits messages into chunks and runs the full analysis prompt on each chunk
2. Merges responses via `mergeAnalysisResponses()` (deduplicates decisions/learnings)
3. Falls back to `extractFacetsOnly()` when chunked analysis loses holistic facets

With the 3-prompt split:
- **Session Insights** (summary, decisions, learnings, character) still uses the full transcript. For very long sessions exceeding 80k tokens, it **still needs chunking or truncation** for decisions and learnings. The `mergeAnalysisResponses()` function (line 582) already handles deduplicating decisions/learnings across chunks. Removing chunking entirely would mean very long sessions get truncated (losing middle content) rather than chunked (analyzing all content in segments).
- **Session Facets** already uses first/last 20 messages -- inherently bounded, no chunking needed.
- **PQ** already handles oversized input via truncation (lines 325-327).

**Recommendation:** Replace the chunking fallback-to-facets logic (lines 172-211) -- that part is cleanly eliminated. But keep the chunking-for-insights logic (lines 129-170) as a fallback for very long sessions, or adopt PQ's simpler truncation approach for insights too. The trade-off: truncation loses middle-of-session decisions; chunking preserves them but at higher token cost. For a knowledge capture prompt, I'd lean toward keeping chunking for insights.

### 3. Dashboard Integration

**Verdict: VALIDATED -- moderate change, not large**

The dashboard currently has two independent paths:

**Path A: SSE Streaming** (primary UX) -- `AnalysisContext.tsx`
- `startAnalysis(session, type)` takes a type of `'session' | 'prompt_quality'`
- Maps to either `/api/analysis/session/stream` or `/api/analysis/prompt-quality/stream`
- One analysis at a time (guarded by `isAnalyzingRef`)

**Path B: Direct POST** (bulk analysis) -- `BulkAnalyzeButton.tsx` and `useAnalysis.ts`
- Calls `analyzeSession(sessionId)` via `/api/analysis/session` POST

For the 3-prompt architecture, the dashboard change is **small in scope but important in design**:

1. **Option A: Server orchestrates.** The dashboard still calls one endpoint (e.g., `/api/analysis/full`). The server internally runs all 3 prompts via `Promise.all()` and streams composite progress. Dashboard code barely changes -- just update SSE event types.

2. **Option B: Dashboard orchestrates.** The dashboard fires 3 parallel SSE streams or POST calls. This requires significant changes to `AnalysisContext.tsx` -- tracking 3 concurrent states, compositing progress, handling partial failure in UI.

**Recommendation: Option A (server orchestrates).** The dashboard already has a "one analysis at a time" model with toast notifications. Having the server `Promise.all()` the three calls and emit composite SSE events preserves the current UX pattern. The dashboard only needs the AnalysisState `type` field updated (from `'session' | 'prompt_quality'` to `'full' | 'session' | 'prompt_quality'`) and a new menu item for "Analyze All."

### 4. `session_character` Ownership

**Verdict: VALIDATED -- Session Insights is the right home**

`session_character` classification requires understanding the full session arc -- what was the user doing across the entire conversation? The 7 character types (deep_focus, bug_hunt, feature_build, etc.) are defined by message count patterns, tool usage, and overall intent.

The Session Insights prompt already sees the full transcript and is the "senior staff engineer" who understands the session holistically. The facet prompt sees only first/last 20 messages -- it lacks the middle of the conversation that distinguishes, say, `deep_focus` (50+ messages going deep) from `feature_build` (file creation throughout).

**No concerns about the facet prompt needing session character context.** The facet prompt classifies behavioral patterns (friction, effective patterns) which are category-based and don't depend on session character. The `workflow_pattern` field in facets (plan-then-implement, iterative-refinement, etc.) is a different axis from session character.

### 5. Save Order and Error Handling

**Verdict: CONCERN -- partial failure needs a defined policy**

Currently, `analyzeSession()` saves atomically-ish: insights first, then facets, then session character update (lines 250-266). If facets fail, insights are still saved. This is already a partial-success pattern.

With 3 independent calls, the failure modes expand:
- Insights succeed, facets fail, PQ succeeds
- Insights fail, facets succeed, PQ succeeds
- Any single call fails

**Key question: is partial success acceptable?**

Yes, and it already is. The current code saves insights even if facets fail (line 257: `if (analysisResponse.facets)`). The dashboard already handles sessions with insights but no facets, or insights but no PQ. Each data type is independently queryable.

**Recommendation:** Adopt a "best-effort all, report failures" pattern. `Promise.allSettled()` (not `Promise.all()`) runs all 3, saves whatever succeeded, and returns a composite result showing which succeeded and which failed. The dashboard toast can say "2 of 3 analyses complete" with the option to retry the failed one. This is a better UX than all-or-nothing.

The one thing to get right: **`deleteSessionInsights()` must only run after its corresponding save succeeds.** Currently, `analyzeSession()` saves new insights then deletes old non-PQ insights (lines 250-254). If insights and PQ are now independent, each must do its own save-then-cleanup independently. This is already the case in the current code -- `analyzeSession()` excludes PQ from deletion, `analyzePromptQuality()` only deletes PQ types.

### 6. Backfill Unification

**Verdict: CONCERN -- `extractFacetsOnly()` is almost suitable but needs enhancement**

Comparing the two facet extraction approaches:

**Session analysis prompt facets** (embedded in `SESSION_ANALYSIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, lines 263-299 of prompts.ts):
- Full transcript input
- Detailed friction classification guidance with `_reasoning` CoT
- Effective pattern classification guidance with driver decision tree and baseline exclusion
- References `FRICTION_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` and `EFFECTIVE_PATTERN_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` shared constants

**`FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT`** (line 625 of prompts.ts):
- Summary + first/last 20 messages
- Same canonical categories via template literals (`CANONICAL_FRICTION_CATEGORIES.join(', ')`)
- References the same `FRICTION_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` and `EFFECTIVE_PATTERN_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` shared constants
- Same JSON schema shape

The two prompts are **functionally equivalent in guidance quality**. They use the same shared constants for classification guidance (`FRICTION_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE`, `EFFECTIVE_PATTERN_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE`). The only difference is input scope (full transcript vs first/last 20 messages).

**Enhancement needed:** The `extractFacetsOnly()` function (line 882) currently returns `{ success: boolean; error?: string }` -- it doesn't return usage data or the extracted facets themselves. For the 3-prompt architecture, the facet call should return usage data for composite token reporting. This is a small change (add `usage` to the return type, propagate from the LLM response).

The claim that backfill becomes "re-run the facet call" is **valid**. The existing `extractFacetsOnly()` is already the backfill function -- it's called by the backfill endpoint. Making it the primary facet extraction path just means every analysis uses the same code path that backfill already uses. This is actually a simplification and a win for consistency.

### 7. API Route Changes

**Verdict: VALIDATED -- needs a new composite endpoint**

Current routes in `server/src/routes/analysis.ts`:
- `POST /api/analysis/session` -- runs `analyzeSession()`, returns result
- `GET /api/analysis/session/stream` -- SSE streaming for session analysis
- `POST /api/analysis/prompt-quality` -- runs `analyzePromptQuality()`
- `GET /api/analysis/prompt-quality/stream` -- SSE streaming for PQ
- `POST /api/analysis/recurring` -- recurring insight detection

**For the 3-prompt architecture:**

The existing endpoints can remain (for individual re-analysis and backward compatibility). Add:

1. **`POST /api/analysis/full`** -- runs all 3 in parallel, returns composite result
2. **`GET /api/analysis/full/stream`** -- SSE streaming with composite progress

The `full/stream` endpoint would:
1. Load session + messages once (avoid 3x DB reads)
2. Run `Promise.allSettled([analyzeSession(...), extractFacetsOnly(...), analyzePromptQuality(...)])`
3. Emit progress events for each leg
4. Emit a composite completion event

The existing individual endpoints stay for: re-analyzing just PQ, re-analyzing just session insights, and the "Analyze Session" / "Analyze Prompt Quality" dropdown items in the dashboard.

**Key consideration for the route:** The session + messages DB query is duplicated across the 3 existing endpoints (lines 28-42, 92-104, 198-210). The composite endpoint should fetch once and pass the same data to all 3 functions.

---

### Summary Table

| Review Point | Verdict | Action Needed |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Parallel execution | VALIDATED | None -- safe within single Node.js process |
| 2. Chunking simplification | VALIDATED with nuance | Keep chunking for insights (long sessions), remove facet fallback from chunking path |
| 3. Dashboard integration | VALIDATED | Server-side orchestration preferred; small dashboard change |
| 4. session_character ownership | VALIDATED | Session Insights is correct home |
| 5. Save order / error handling | CONCERN | Use `Promise.allSettled()`, report partial success, keep per-type cleanup independent |
| 6. Backfill unification | VALIDATED with minor gap | Add usage tracking to `extractFacetsOnly()` return type |
| 7. API route changes | VALIDATED | Add `/api/analysis/full` + `/api/analysis/full/stream`, keep existing endpoints |

### Recommendations

1. **Server orchestrates, not dashboard.** Add a `POST /api/analysis/full` and `GET /api/analysis/full/stream` endpoint. The dashboard's "Analyze" button fires this single endpoint. The server runs `Promise.allSettled()` on the 3 calls and streams composite progress. This minimizes dashboard changes and keeps the analysis orchestration logic server-side where it belongs.

2. **Keep chunking for insights.** The insights prompt (summary + decisions + learnings) benefits from seeing the full conversation, especially for decisions made mid-session. For sessions exceeding 80k tokens, chunking preserves mid-session content. The facet and PQ prompts already handle bounded/truncated input.

3. **`Promise.allSettled()` over `Promise.all()`.** Partial success is the right default. A PQ failure (e.g., <2 user messages) should not prevent insights and facets from being saved.

4. **Enhance `extractFacetsOnly()` return type.** Add `usage?: { inputTokens: number; outputTokens: number }` to support composite token reporting.

5. **Single DB fetch in composite endpoint.** Load session + messages once, pass the same data objects to all 3 analysis functions. The current route code duplicates the session/messages query -- the composite endpoint eliminates that.

Now I have a thorough understanding of the code. Here is my review.

---

## Independent LLM Architecture Review: 2-Call vs 3-Call Session Analysis

### 1. Valid Concerns

**The first/last 20 messages approach has a real information gap -- and it matters more than the team thinks.**

Reading the actual `FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` and `generateFacetOnlyPrompt()` (lines 625-690 of prompts.ts), the prompt explicitly says "You will receive a session summary and a sample of messages (first and last from the conversation)." The prompt is honest about the limitation, which means the LLM knows it's working with incomplete data. But knowing about a gap doesn't fill it.

The critical issue: friction attribution requires causal reasoning. The `FRICTION_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` (lines 85-113) includes a 3-step decision tree: Is it external? Could the user have prevented it? Did the AI fail despite clear input? Steps 2 and 3 require understanding the FULL interaction context -- what the user said, how the AI interpreted it, and what went wrong. If the friction event occurs at message #25 in a 50-message session, the facet prompt literally cannot execute its own decision tree correctly. It will either hallucinate an attribution (bad) or default to a safe label (inaccurate).

This is not a theoretical concern. Looking at the session analysis prompt (lines 259-367), the current `SESSION_ANALYSIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` explicitly places facets as "PART 1" and says "extract these first as a holistic session assessment." The word "holistic" is doing real work -- it means the LLM reads the full transcript, forms a mental model of the entire session arc, THEN classifies friction and patterns. The proposed 3-call architecture replaces this with a reader that sees the bookends of the conversation.

**Ollama users will experience 3x latency, not "zero cost parallelism."**

The team's parallelism claim assumes cloud API endpoints. For Ollama users (single GPU, sequential inference), 3 calls = 3 serial LLM inferences. If the current 2-call architecture takes ~40 seconds on a local model, the 3-call architecture takes ~60 seconds. This is a 50% regression for a user segment that the tool explicitly supports (`createLLMClient` abstracts over OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, and Ollama).

**The "same prompt for primary and backfill" collapses two different quality tiers.**

The `extractFacetsOnly()` function (lines 882-937 of analysis.ts) currently takes `session.summary` as input. The `generateFacetOnlyPrompt()` has `${sessionSummary ? \`Session Summary: ${sessionSummary}\n\` : ''}` -- note the conditional. If there's no summary, the facet prompt runs with ONLY the first/last 20 messages and no narrative anchor.

In the current architecture, this is fine: backfill only runs on sessions that already have insights (the `/api/facets/missing` endpoint finds sessions with insights but no facets). So `session.summary` is always populated.

In the proposed architecture, if the 3 calls run in parallel, Call 2 (facets) runs BEFORE Call 1 (insights) finishes. There is no summary yet. The facet prompt runs blind -- first/last 20 messages with no summary. This is strictly worse than the current backfill path which always has a summary available.

**Batch backfill at 3x API calls creates real throttling risk.**

The backfill endpoint (lines 163-272 of facets.ts) processes up to 200 sessions sequentially. Currently it makes 1 API call per session. If primary analysis now requires 3 calls per session, and a user re-analyzes multiple sessions (e.g., after taxonomy changes, which has happened 5 times in the project history), that's 600 API calls instead of 200. Most API providers enforce per-minute rate limits (Anthropic: 50-60 RPM on free tier, OpenAI: 60-500 RPM depending on tier). At 3 parallel calls per session, the effective session-processing rate drops by 3x against rate limits.

### 2. Overblown Concerns

**"Persona tension causes quality degradation" is likely not a real problem.**

The current `SESSION_ANALYSIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` persona is "a senior staff engineer writing entries for a team's engineering knowledge base." The facets section asks this same persona to assess friction and patterns. There is no actual persona conflict here -- a senior staff engineer absolutely assesses session quality as part of knowledge capture. The persona is coherent.

What the team might be observing as "tension" is actually prompt length pressure. The current system prompt is ~370 lines with dense classification guidance (friction categories, pattern categories, decision trees, contrastive pairs, baseline exclusions). Adding more guidance to an already long prompt causes quality degradation not because of persona conflict, but because of attention dilution. The fix for attention dilution is prompt restructuring, not call splitting.

**"The current approach was tuned across 5 PRs and splitting means re-tuning" is overstated.**

Reading the prompts, the tuning has been on the TAXONOMY itself (friction categories, pattern categories, attribution decision trees, driver decision trees). This taxonomy is expressed as shared constants (`FRICTION_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE`, `EFFECTIVE_PATTERN_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE`) that are imported into both the session analysis prompt AND the facet-only prompt. A 3-call split doesn't invalidate the taxonomy work -- it ports forward.

**"Cost increase of +7% is concerning" is not a real problem.**

The cost is dominated by input tokens (the full transcript). Call 1 and Call 3 both need the full transcript regardless of architecture. Call 2 uses first/last 20 messages (~3k tokens). The marginal cost of Call 2 is small. HTTP overhead per call is milliseconds compared to seconds of LLM inference. The 7% estimate is probably right and genuinely negligible.

### 3. Missing Considerations

**The chunked-session path already does exactly what the 3-call architecture proposes -- and it reveals the quality problem.**

Lines 172-211 of analysis.ts show the chunked path: when a session exceeds 80k input tokens, the code already splits facets into a separate call using `FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` with first/last 20 messages. This means the team already has empirical data on how the lightweight facet prompt performs vs the full-transcript approach. The fact that it was designed as a FALLBACK for chunked sessions (where you literally cannot pass the full transcript) suggests the team considered it a compromise, not an ideal approach.

If the team has telemetry or quality data comparing facet quality between chunked (lightweight) and non-chunked (full transcript) sessions, that data should drive this decision. If they don't have that data, they should collect it before committing to the 3-call architecture.

**The proposed architecture has an ordering dependency it pretends doesn't exist.**

If all 3 calls run in parallel, Call 2 (facets) has no summary to work with. Two options:
1. Run Call 1 first, wait for summary, then run Call 2 -- which means Calls 1 and 2 are sequential, not parallel. Only Calls 2 and 3 can be parallel (or 1 and 3).
2. Run Call 2 without a summary -- which degrades quality since the summary provides the narrative anchor for facet classification.

Neither option delivers the claimed "3 parallel calls" architecture cleanly.

**There's a simpler alternative nobody seems to be considering: keep 2 calls, restructure the prompt.**

The actual problem (if there is one) is that the session analysis prompt is overloaded -- it asks for knowledge capture AND behavioral diagnostics in one output. But the solution doesn't have to be call splitting. You could restructure the prompt to produce output in two phases within the same response:
1. First emit facets (PART 1 -- which it already does)
2. Then emit insights (PART 2 -- which it already does)

The current prompt ALREADY does this. The "PART 1" / "PART 2" structure in the system prompt (lines 263-366) is explicitly designed for this. If the LLM attention is diluted across too many output fields, the fix is to simplify the output schema (fewer fields per facet, or reduce max items), not to add another API call.

**session_character lands in a weird place in the 3-call architecture.**

Currently, `session_character` (deep_focus, bug_hunt, etc.) is extracted in Call 1 alongside insights. In the proposed split, it stays in Call 1. But session character classification depends on tool call patterns and conversation dynamics -- the same signals the facet prompt needs. If you split facets out, should session_character go with facets (it's a session-level assessment) or stay with insights (it's metadata)? Neither home is perfect.

**Error handling and partial failure modes get more complicated with 3 calls.**

Currently: Call 1 fails -> no insights, no facets. Call 2 fails -> no PQ. Simple.

With 3 calls: What if Call 2 (facets) fails but Calls 1 and 3 succeed? You have insights and PQ but no facets. Is that a success? Do you retry just Call 2? The current codebase has no partial-retry mechanism. Adding one is non-trivial and error-prone.

### 4. My Verdict

**The 3-call architecture is solving a problem that hasn't been demonstrated to exist, while introducing a real quality regression.**

Here's my reasoning:

1. **The information loss is real and unrecoverable.** Friction attribution and pattern driver classification require understanding the full session narrative. First/last 20 messages is a structural limitation that no amount of prompt engineering can overcome for mid-session events. The team has invested 5 PRs in building a sophisticated attribution decision tree -- and the proposed architecture prevents the LLM from executing that decision tree correctly for a meaningful fraction of sessions.

2. **The "persona tension" motivation is unfounded.** I see no evidence that the current dual-purpose prompt produces lower quality than a split approach would. The team should A/B test this claim before rebuilding the architecture around it.

3. **The parallelism benefit is user-segment dependent.** Cloud API users get latency reduction. Ollama users get latency regression. The product explicitly supports both.

**What I would do instead:**

- **Keep 2 calls.** The current architecture is simpler, gives facets full transcript access, and has been tuned over 5 PRs.
- **If prompt length is genuinely causing quality issues**, restructure the output schema to reduce the number of fields the LLM must fill, rather than splitting into more calls. For example: reduce max friction_points from 5 to 3 (most sessions don't have 5 real friction moments anyway), or remove the `_reasoning` field from the output and rely on the decision tree guidance in the system prompt to produce correct classifications.
- **If you must split calls**, make it 2 calls with a different split: Call 1 = full transcript -> insights + facets (current), Call 2 = full transcript -> PQ (current). This is literally what you already have. It works.
- **If you absolutely insist on separating facets**, pass the full transcript to the facet call, not first/last 20 messages. At that point you're paying for 3 full-transcript calls, the cost increase is ~50% not 7%, and you should think hard about whether that's worth it.

The strongest version of the 3-call proposal would be: run Call 1 (insights + session_character, full transcript) first, THEN in parallel run Call 2 (facets with summary + full transcript) and Call 3 (PQ with full transcript). This preserves the summary dependency, keeps full context for facets, and gets partial parallelism (total wall-clock = Call 1 + max(Call 2, Call 3)). But even this is marginal improvement over the current approach -- you're adding architectural complexity for maybe 30% latency reduction on the second phase.

Three very different perspectives came back. Let me present them clearly — the fresh reviewer in particular raises points the team missed.

## Summary: All Three Reviews

### TA — "Proceed, with refinements"

| Point | Verdict |
|-------|---------|
| Parallel execution safe | Validated — single Node.js process, WAL mode, no contention |
| Chunking simplification | Validated — remove facet fallback, **keep chunking for insights** (long sessions) |
| Dashboard integration | Server orchestrates via new `/api/analysis/full/stream` endpoint |
| session_character in Insights | Validated — needs full transcript, not first/last 20 |
| Partial failure | Use `Promise.allSettled()`, save whatever succeeds |
| Backfill unification | Validated — add usage tracking to `extractFacetsOnly()` return |

**TA says go ahead.** Recommends server-side orchestration, single DB fetch, `Promise.allSettled()`.

---

### Cofounder — "Proceed. This resolves all my concerns."

> *"Single progress state. One spinner, one completion. Never expose the three-pipeline split in default UX."*

Three implementation rules:
1. Single progress state — one spinner, one completion toast
2. Re-run goes in overflow/kebab menu, not separate buttons
3. "Facets" never appears in UI copy — users see "Patterns & Friction"

**Cofounder says go ahead.**

---

### Fresh LLM Reviewer — "Hold on. You're solving a problem that hasn't been demonstrated."

This is the critical counterpoint. Several strong challenges:

**Challenge 1: The Information Gap is Real**

> *"If the friction event occurs at message #25 in a 50-message session, the facet prompt literally cannot execute its own decision tree correctly."*

The facet-only prompt sees first/last 20 messages. For a 50-message session, messages 21-30 are invisible. Friction attribution requires **causal reasoning** about WHY something went wrong — if the root cause is in the middle, the facet prompt will either hallucinate an attribution or default to a safe label.

The current session insights prompt sees EVERYTHING and classifies facets with full context. The word "holistic" in the prompt is doing real work.

**Challenge 2: The Summary Dependency Kills True Parallelism**

> *"If all 3 calls run in parallel, Call 2 (facets) has no summary to work with."*

`generateFacetOnlyPrompt()` uses `session.summary` as a narrative anchor. If facets run in parallel with insights, there IS no summary yet. Either:
- Run insights first → facets/PQ parallel (sequential + parallel, not fully parallel)
- Run facets without summary → quality degradation

**Challenge 3: Ollama Users Get 3x Latency, Not Zero**

Single GPU = sequential inference. 3 calls = ~60s instead of ~40s. The "zero latency" claim only holds for cloud API users.

**Challenge 4: Persona Tension May Not Be Real**

> *"A senior staff engineer absolutely assesses session quality as part of knowledge capture. The persona is coherent."*

The reviewer argues what the team calls "persona tension" might actually be **attention dilution from prompt length** — fixable by simplifying the output schema, not by splitting calls.

**Challenge 5: A Simpler Alternative Exists**

> *"Keep 2 calls. If you must separate facets, pass the full transcript to the facet call."*

If facets get the full transcript, cost is +50% not +7%. But quality is preserved. The reviewer's preferred approach:

```
Call 1 (insights + session_character, full transcript) 
    → then in parallel →
Call 2 (facets, full transcript + summary from Call 1)
Call 3 (PQ, full transcript)
```

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The fresh reviewer identifies a fundamental tension the team glossed over:** The +7% cost claim depends on the facet call being lightweight (first/last 20 messages). But the quality argument for splitting depends on each prompt having clean single-purpose focus. If lightweight input degrades facet quality, you either accept worse facets or send the full transcript — which makes cost +50%, not +7%.

The existing chunked-session fallback (lines 172-211 in `analysis.ts`) already uses the lightweight facet prompt, but it was **designed as a compromise for sessions too large to process** — not as the ideal approach. Promoting a fallback to primary status deserves scrutiny.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

---

## The Decision Matrix

| Concern | 3-call (lightweight facets) | 3-call (full transcript facets) | Keep 2-call (status quo) |
|---------|---------------------------|-------------------------------|------------------------|
| Facet quality | **Degraded** (middle messages lost) | Preserved | Preserved |
| Persona separation | Clean | Clean | Dual-mode |
| Cost | +7% | +50% | Baseline |
| Ollama latency | +50% | +100% | Baseline |
| Parallelism | Partial (summary dependency) | Partial | Current |
| Code simplicity | Simpler | Similar complexity | Current |
| Backfill consistency | One path | One path | Two paths |

---

This is a genuine three-way disagreement. The TA and Cofounder say proceed, the fresh reviewer says the foundation assumption (lightweight facets are good enough) may be wrong and you should prove it before committing.

**The pivotal question:** Is the first/last 20 messages approach good enough for friction attribution, or does it produce meaningfully worse facets than the full transcript? You have empirical data — sessions that went through the chunked path (which uses the lightweight facet prompt) vs non-chunked sessions. 

How do you want to proceed?

Good. So `session.summary` comes from the source tool (Claude Code's own summary field) populated at sync time. It exists before any LLM analysis call. This is important for the parallelism question.

Now I have the full picture. Let me write the honest reassessment.

---

# Revised Assessment: Honest Engagement with the Critique

## 1. Is the Information Gap Real?

**Yes. The fresh reviewer is right, and I understated this problem.**

Let me be specific about what "first/last 20 messages" actually misses. For a 50-message session:

- Messages 1-20 = seen (session setup, initial approach, early execution)
- Messages 21-30 = **invisible** (this is typically where friction lives -- the mid-session pivots, wrong approaches discovered, debugging dead ends)
- Messages 31-50 = seen (resolution, final implementation, wrap-up)

Friction attribution requires causal reasoning: "User#14 gave a vague instruction, which caused Assistant#15 to go down wrong path A, which wasn't discovered until User#28 said 'that's not right,' leading to 10 messages of rework." If messages 21-30 contain the discovery of the wrong path and the user's correction, the facet prompt sees the vague instruction (message 14, in the first 20) and the final fix (in the last 20) but cannot trace the causal chain through the middle.

What happens then? The model does one of two things:
- **Hallucinated attribution:** It infers a causal story that fits the bookends. This is the worst outcome -- the `_reasoning` CoT will sound plausible but is fabricated.
- **Conservative defaulting:** It classifies less confidently, tends toward "ai-capability" (the safe default when you can't see what went wrong), and produces fewer friction points.

Neither is acceptable for a feature whose entire value proposition is accurate behavioral diagnostics.

**How much is actually lost?** For typical sessions:
- Short sessions (<25 messages): first/last 20 covers everything. No loss.
- Medium sessions (25-40 messages): first/last 20 overlaps, missing ~0-10 messages. Moderate loss.
- Long sessions (40-80 messages): missing 0-40 messages (0-50%). Significant loss.
- Very long sessions (80+): missing 40+ messages (50%+). Severe loss.

The median session is probably ~40 messages. That means the lightweight prompt misses roughly 10-20% of conversation content for the median case. But friction events cluster in the middle of sessions (that's when things go wrong and get corrected), so the information value of those missed messages is disproportionately high -- probably 30-40% of the friction-relevant content.

**My previous claim that "the facet call is lightweight because it already uses the summary + first/last 20 message pattern" was technically true but misleading.** The backfill prompt was designed as a *compromise* for sessions that already had insights. Promoting a compromise to primary status is exactly what the reviewer flagged.

## 2. Was the +7% Cost Claim Wrong?

**Yes, it was misleading as presented.**

The +7% figure is accurate only if the facet call stays lightweight (first/last 20 messages). But point 1 above establishes that lightweight facets produce meaningfully worse output for medium-to-long sessions. If facets need the full transcript:

| Architecture | Conversation tokens sent | Fixed overhead | Est. Total |
|-------------|------------------------|----------------|------------|
| Current (2 calls) | 30,000 (15k x 2) | ~3,600 | ~36,600 |
| 3 calls, full transcript | 45,000 (15k x 3) | ~4,700 | ~52,700 |
| Delta | +15,000 | +1,100 | **+44%** |

At Sonnet pricing: $0.0015 current vs $0.0022 proposed. That's +47%, not +7%.

This is still cheap in absolute terms ($0.0007/session, or 7 cents per 100 sessions). But I presented it as a negligible increase when it's actually a meaningful jump. The cost doesn't kill the proposal, but I should have been upfront about the real number rather than optimizing the presentation around the lightweight variant.

## 3. Is "Persona Tension" Real, or Is It Attention Dilution?

**The fresh reviewer is mostly right. I overstated persona tension.**

Let me be honest about what I claimed: that the "senior staff engineer" persona biases friction attribution toward "ai-capability" because it "empathizes with the developer." That's speculative -- I have no empirical evidence that the persona label causes systematic attribution bias. What I actually have evidence for is:

- The `collaborative` driver is over-selected by smaller models (documented in memory from PR #129 review)
- The `_reasoning` CoT was added because classification quality was poor (documented in PR #138)

Neither of these are caused by persona tension. They're caused by:
1. **Attention dilution:** The system prompt is ~2,350 tokens of instructions covering two fundamentally different output structures (narrative insights vs structured facets). The model's attention budget is split.
2. **Output schema complexity:** The model must produce a JSON response with 7+ distinct top-level structures, some requiring multi-step decision trees. More output fields = more chances for the model to cut corners on later fields.
3. **Instruction ordering effects:** Facets are "PART 1" but the examples (lines 338-365) are all insight examples. There are no facet examples. The model sees detailed quality bars for insights but not for facets.

The fresh reviewer's reframe is correct: a senior staff engineer absolutely assesses session quality as part of knowledge capture. The persona is coherent. The problem isn't WHO is doing the assessment -- it's that the prompt is asking for too many things at once.

**This is important because it changes the solution.** If the problem is persona tension, you need separate calls. If the problem is attention dilution and prompt length, you can fix it by restructuring the single prompt -- trimming output fields, adding facet examples, reordering instructions.

## 4. The CoT + Full Context Argument

The user's point here is strong and connects directly to how `_reasoning` works in practice.

The `_reasoning` field forces the model to walk through a decision tree:
- Friction: "Step 1: Is the cause external? Step 2: Could the USER have prevented this? Step 3: ..."
- Patterns: "Step 1: Did user infrastructure enable this? Step 2: Did the user explicitly request it? ..."

This decision tree requires examining specific evidence from the conversation. "Could the user have prevented this?" demands looking at what the user actually said. "Did user infrastructure enable this?" demands checking whether CLAUDE.md rules or hooks were involved.

With first/last 20 messages, the CoT becomes: "Step 2: Could the user have prevented this? I can see the user's initial prompt and the final outcome, but I cannot see the intermediate interactions where the friction occurred. Based on available evidence..." This is a model reasoning confidently from incomplete evidence -- arguably worse than no CoT, because it produces structured justifications for potentially wrong conclusions.

**CoT amplifies the information gap problem.** Without CoT, the model might hedge with a lower confidence score. With CoT, the model is forced to commit to a reasoning chain, and that chain will be built on whatever evidence is visible. Partial evidence + forced reasoning = confidently wrong attributions.

## 5. Revised Recommendation

Given the critique, here's where each option actually stands:

**Option A (status quo, 2 calls):** Facets get full context, insights and facets share the same call, PQ is separate. The attention dilution problem is real but manageable. This is the known-good baseline.

**Option B (3 calls, full transcript for facets):** Solves attention dilution. Costs +47%. But the core question is: does splitting into separate calls actually produce better facets than restructuring the existing prompt? I don't have evidence that it does. The quality problems we've seen (collaborative over-selection, missing `_reasoning`) are caused by instruction complexity, not by co-presence with insight extraction.

**Option C (3 calls sequential):** Insights first, then facets+PQ. This works mechanically -- `session.summary` is already available from sync time (it comes from the source tool, not from LLM generation), so facets don't need to wait for insights. But this means facets still need the full transcript (for the reasons in point 1), which negates the cost saving that made the 3-call architecture attractive.

**Option D (2 calls restructured):** Address attention dilution without adding calls. This is what the critique actually points toward.

**Option E: 2 calls, but restructure the session analysis prompt.**

Here is what I recommend.

### Recommendation: Option D -- Restructure, Don't Split

The diagnosis from my previous review was partially right (there IS a quality problem with facet classification) but the prescription was wrong (splitting into separate calls doesn't address the root cause and introduces new problems).

The actual root causes of facet quality issues are:
1. No facet examples in the prompt (insight examples get 28 lines, facet examples get 0)
2. Output schema asks for too many fields in one response
3. Classification guidance is injected as raw text blocks, not structured around examples
4. The "PART 1 / PART 2" ordering puts facets first but examples last

**What a restructured 2-call prompt would change:**

1. **Add 1-2 facet examples** at the quality bar level of the insight examples. Show what a good friction_point with `_reasoning` looks like with full context reasoning, not just the schema. This is the single highest-impact change -- ~150 tokens, massive quality improvement for facet classification.

2. **Move the classification guidance blocks inline with their schema definitions** instead of appending them via template interpolation. Currently `FRICTION_CLASSIFICATION_GUIDANCE` is injected as a wall of text after the friction_points field definition. Interleaving guidance with the schema it applies to improves adherence.

3. **Trim the insight examples.** The two example blocks (lines 338-365) are 27 lines / ~700 tokens. They're excellent examples, but the model has seen enough similar patterns in training. One shorter example would suffice, freeing ~350 tokens for facet examples.

4. **Consider dropping `session_character` from the LLM call.** It's a 7-way classification that can be done deterministically from message count + tool call patterns (your `detectSessionCharacter` function in `titles.ts` already exists). Removing it from the LLM output reduces output complexity by one field.

This approach:
- Keeps facets in the full-transcript call (no information gap)
- Costs zero additional API calls
- Addresses attention dilution directly (better examples, clearer structure)
- Maintains CoT effectiveness (full context for reasoning)
- Preserves the existing backfill architecture unchanged
- Is a much smaller change to implement (~50 lines of prompt edits)

### When to Reconsider the 3-Call Split

The split becomes worth it if:
- You add monitoring that shows facet quality is still poor AFTER prompt restructuring (measure attribution accuracy against human-reviewed ground truth on 20-30 sessions)
- The session insights prompt grows further (if it crosses ~3,000 system tokens, attention dilution becomes harder to fix with restructuring alone)
- You add new facet fields that increase output complexity beyond what one call can handle cleanly

But you should try the cheaper fix first and measure before adding architectural complexity.

## What I Got Wrong

1. **I overstated persona tension.** The "senior staff engineer" persona is coherent with facet extraction. The real problem is prompt length and instruction density, not role conflict.

2. **I presented the +7% cost as the real number** when it was only accurate for the lightweight variant, which I was simultaneously recommending. If facets need full context (and they do), the cost is +47%. I should have led with that number.

3. **I promoted the backfill prompt to primary status** without adequately scrutinizing its design constraints. It was built as a fallback for chunked sessions and sessions needing backfill -- not as the ideal facet extraction approach.

4. **I understated the information gap.** "40% of conversation" underestimates the problem because friction-relevant content is disproportionately concentrated in the middle of sessions.

5. **I framed parallelism as a pure win** without acknowledging that the `session.summary` used in the facet prompt comes from sync-time (source tool summary), not LLM-generated summary. This means parallelism does technically work, but the facet prompt is then working from a potentially low-quality source tool summary (e.g., Claude Code sessions often have null summaries) rather than the richer narrative summary the LLM would generate. The quality difference matters.

The fresh reviewer was right on the substantive points. Option D (restructure the existing prompt) addresses the real problems without introducing new ones.

## LLM Expert's Revised Position: "I Was Wrong on Several Points"

### What They Retracted

| Previous Claim | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Persona tension causes quality degradation" | **Overstated.** The persona is coherent. The real problem is attention dilution from prompt length + output complexity |
| "+7% cost increase" | **Misleading.** Only true with lightweight facets. With full transcript: **+47%** |
| "Lightweight facets are good enough" | **Wrong.** Backfill prompt was designed as a fallback, not an ideal approach. Friction events cluster in mid-session — exactly where first/last 20 messages creates a blind spot |
| "Parallelism is a pure win" | **Incomplete.** Ollama = sequential. Summary dependency exists. |

### The Root Cause Reframe

The Expert now agrees with the fresh reviewer: the quality problems aren't from persona tension — they're from **attention dilution**:

1. **No facet examples** — insight examples get 28 lines, facet examples get 0
2. **Output schema asks too much** — 7+ top-level structures in one response
3. **Classification guidance is injected as raw text walls** — not interleaved with schemas
4. **PART 1/PART 2 ordering is backwards** — facets first, but examples are all for insights

### The CoT Argument Clinches It

> *"CoT amplifies the information gap problem. Without CoT, the model might hedge. With CoT, the model is forced to commit to a reasoning chain built on partial evidence. Partial evidence + forced reasoning = confidently wrong attributions."*

This is the strongest argument for keeping facets in the full-transcript call. The `_reasoning` field was added across PRs #129 and #138 specifically to improve classification quality. It only works when the model can see the complete causal chain.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The diagnostic was right, the prescription was wrong.** The session insights prompt IS overloaded — that's a real observation. But the fix isn't "move facets to a different call" — it's "make the existing prompt better at facets." The Expert identifies a specific high-impact change: adding 1-2 facet examples (~150 tokens) to match the insight examples that already exist. The model currently gets detailed quality bars for insights but zero examples for facets — it's no wonder facets are the weaker output.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### New Recommendation: Option D — Restructure, Don't Split

**Keep 2 calls. Fix the real problems in the session insights prompt:**

| Change | Impact | Tokens |
|--------|--------|--------|
| Add 1-2 facet examples (friction with `_reasoning`, attribution) | Highest impact — model currently has zero facet examples | +150 |
| Move classification guidance inline with schema definitions | Better instruction adherence | ±0 |
| Trim insight examples (2 → 1 shorter example) | Free attention budget for facets | -350 |
| Consider dropping `session_character` from LLM (deterministic detection exists) | Reduces output complexity | -50 output |

**Why this is better than splitting:**
- Facets keep full transcript (no information gap)
- Zero additional API calls (no cost increase)
- CoT reasoning has full context to work with
- ~50 lines of prompt edits vs architectural restructuring
- Backfill architecture stays unchanged

### When to Reconsider Splitting

The Expert says split into 3 calls **only if:**
- Facet quality is still poor AFTER prompt restructuring (measure against 20-30 human-reviewed sessions)
- The system prompt grows past ~3,000 tokens
- New facet fields increase output complexity beyond one call's capacity

---

## Where We've Landed After 4 Rounds

| Round | Proposal | Outcome |
|-------|----------|---------|
| 1 | Move facets from insights → PQ | Rejected — persona bias, loses AI/environmental friction |
| 2 | Dual-write (both produce facets) | Rejected — worst quality, wasted tokens |
| 3 | 3 calls parallel (lightweight facets) | Challenged — information gap, false cost claim |
| 4 | **Restructure existing prompt** | Expert's revised recommendation |

The original concern (PQ data feeding into Reflect) can still be addressed separately via **Option B from round 1** — feed PQ aggregates into Reflect as a complementary signal (~30 lines in `shared-aggregation.ts` + `reflect-prompts.ts`). That's orthogonal to the prompt restructuring.

**Two independent improvements on the table:**
1. **Restructure session insights prompt** — add facet examples, reorder guidance, trim insight examples (quality fix)
2. **Wire PQ aggregates into Reflect** — PQ deficit counts enrich weekly synthesis (data enrichment)
3. **Add `--prompt-quality` flag to backfill** — bulk re-run PQ analysis (original ask)

   878→/**
   879→ * Extract facets only for a session that already has insights (backfill).
   880→ * Uses lightweight prompt with summary + first/last 20 messages.

   882→export async function extractFacetsOnly(
   883→  session: SessionData,
   884→  messages: SQLiteMessageRow[],
   885→  options?: { signal?: AbortSignal }
   886→): Promise<{ success: boolean; error?: string }> {
   887→  if (!isLLMConfigured()) {
   888→    return { success: false, error: 'LLM not configured.' };
   889→  }
   890→
   891→  if (messages.length === 0) {
   892→    return { success: false, error: 'No messages found.' };
   893→  }
   894→
   895→  try {
   896→    const client = createLLMClient();
   897→    const firstMsgs = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages.slice(0, 20));
   898→    const lastMsgs = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages.slice(-20));
   899→    const prompt = generateFacetOnlyPrompt(
   900→      session.project_name,
   901→      session.summary,
   902→      firstMsgs,
   903→      lastMsgs
   904→    );
   905→
   906→    const response = await client.chat([
   907→      { role: 'system', content: FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT },
   908→      { role: 'user', content: prompt },
   909→    ], { signal: options?.signal });
   910→
   911→    const jsonPayload = extractJsonPayload(response.content);
   912→    if (!jsonPayload) {
   913→      return { success: false, error: 'No JSON in facet response.' };
   914→    }
   915→
   916→    let facets: AnalysisResponse['facets'];
   917→    try {
   918→      facets = JSON.parse(jsonPayload);
   919→    } catch {
   920→      facets = JSON.parse(jsonrepair(jsonPayload));
   921→    }
   922→
   923→    if (facets) {
   924→      saveFacetsToDb(session.id, facets, ANALYSIS_VERSION);
   925→    }
   926→
   927→    return { success: true };
   928→  } catch (error) {
   929→    if (error instanceof Error && error.name === 'AbortError') {
   930→      return { success: false, error: 'Cancelled' };
   931→    }
   932→    return {
   933→      success: false,
   934→      error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : 'Facet extraction failed',
   935→    };
   936→  }
   937→}

   100→  session: SessionData,
   101→  messages: SQLiteMessageRow[],
   102→  options?: AnalysisOptions
   103→): Promise<AnalysisResult> {
   104→  if (!isLLMConfigured()) {
   105→    return {
   106→      success: false,
   107→      insights: [],
   108→      error: 'LLM not configured. Run `code-insights config llm` to configure a provider.',
   109→    };
   110→  }
   111→
   112→  if (messages.length === 0) {

   114→      success: false,
   115→      insights: [],
   116→      error: 'No messages found for this session.',
   117→    };
   118→  }
   119→
   120→  try {
   121→    const client = createLLMClient();
   122→    const formattedMessages = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages);
   123→    const estimatedTokens = client.estimateTokens(formattedMessages);
   124→
   125→    let analysisResponse: AnalysisResponse;
   126→    let totalInputTokens = 0;
   127→    let totalOutputTokens = 0;
   128→
   129→    if (estimatedTokens > MAX_INPUT_TOKENS) {
   130→      // Chunk the messages and analyze separately
   131→      const chunks = chunkMessages(messages, client.estimateTokens.bind(client));
   132→      const chunkResponses: AnalysisResponse[] = [];
   133→      const totalChunks = chunks.length;
   134→
   135→      for (let i = 0; i < chunks.length; i++) {
   136→        const chunk = chunks[i];
   137→        options?.onProgress?.({ phase: 'analyzing', currentChunk: i + 1, totalChunks });
   138→
   139→        const chunkFormatted = formatMessagesForAnalysis(chunk);
   140→        const prompt = generateSessionAnalysisPrompt(
   141→          session.project_name,
   142→          session.summary,
   143→          chunkFormatted
   144→        );
   145→
   146→        const response = await client.chat([
   147→          { role: 'system', content: SESSION_ANALYSIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT },
   148→          { role: 'user', content: prompt },
   149→        ], { signal: options?.signal });
   150→
   151→        if (response.usage) {
   152→          totalInputTokens += response.usage.inputTokens;
   153→          totalOutputTokens += response.usage.outputTokens;
   154→        }
   155→
   156→        const parsed = parseAnalysisResponse(response.content);
   157→        if (parsed.success) chunkResponses.push(parsed.data);

   159→
   160→      if (chunkResponses.length === 0) {
   161→        return {
   162→          success: false,
   163→          insights: [],
   164→          error: 'All chunks failed to parse LLM response',
   165→          error_type: 'json_parse_error',

> AGENT

Now I have the complete picture. Here's the answer:

**Yes, the backfill uses a DIFFERENT, lighter prompt with partial conversation data.** They are not the same.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Two completely different code paths exist for facet extraction:**

| | Primary Path (normal analysis) | Backfill Path (`extractFacetsOnly`) |
|---|---|---|
| **System prompt** | `SESSION_ANALYSIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (107 lines — insights + facets combined) | `FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (17 lines — facets only) |
| **Input data** | Full conversation transcript (ALL messages) | Summary + first 20 + last 20 messages |
| **What it produces** | Insights (summary, decisions, learnings) + facets + session_character | Facets only |
| **When it runs** | Dashboard "Analyze" click, or chunked-session fallback | `reflect backfill` CLI command |
| **Code location** | `analyzeSession()` line 214 | `extractFacetsOnly()` line 882 |

The backfill prompt (`analysis.ts:897-898`) explicitly slices messages:
```typescript
const firstMsgs = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages.slice(0, 20));
const lastMsgs = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages.slice(-20));
```

So for a 50-message session, backfill sees messages 1-20 and 31-50 — **messages 21-30 are completely invisible** to it.

The primary path (`analysis.ts:122`) uses everything:
```typescript
const formattedMessages = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages);
```
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

This means **backfill produces lower-quality facets than primary analysis** — by design. It was built as a cost-saving compromise for bulk re-processing sessions that already had insights but lacked facets (before facets existed as a feature).

**Your concern is valid.** If you want backfill to produce the same quality facets as primary analysis, it should use the full conversation, not first/last 20. The trade-off is cost — full transcript backfill for 100 sessions uses ~5x more input tokens than the lightweight approach.

Should the backfill be updated to use the full conversation? Or is the lightweight approach acceptable for bulk re-processing given the cost savings?

> DEVELOPER

This was a blunder. I didn’t realize it. Backfill was introduced to re analyze sessions not save tokens and generate sub standard output.  Fix it first and then build the implementation plan as finalized above. After that come to me with steps for final approval before starting full ceremony implementation

> AGENT

You're right — let me fix the backfill first. The change is in two places: the `extractFacetsOnly()` function (which slices messages) and the backfill endpoint in `facets.ts` (which loads only first/last 20 from DB).

**Changes:**

### 1. `server/src/llm/prompts.ts`
- Update `FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` — remove "sample of messages (first and last from the conversation)"
- Change `generateFacetOnlyPrompt()` — accept full formatted conversation instead of first/last split

### 2. `server/src/llm/analysis.ts`
- Update `extractFacetsOnly()` — format ALL messages, pass to updated prompt function
- Add token estimation + truncation (same pattern PQ uses) as a safety net for very long sessions

### 3. `server/src/routes/facets.ts`
- Update backfill endpoint — load ALL messages instead of first 20 + last 20

### 4. NOT changing:
- The chunked-session fallback (lines 172-211) — these sessions genuinely exceed token limits, first/last 20 is the necessary compromise there
- The `FACET_ONLY_SYSTEM_PROMPT` classification guidance — stays identical

{

  "old_string": "/**\n * Extract facets only for a session that already has insights (backfill).\n * Uses lightweight prompt with summary + first/last 20 messages.\n */\nexport async function extractFacetsOnly(\n  session: SessionData,\n  messages: SQLiteMessageRow[],\n  options?: { signal?: AbortSignal }\n): Promise<{ success: boolean; error?: string }> {\n  if (!isLLMConfigured()) {\n    return { success: false, error: 'LLM not configured.' };\n  }\n\n  if (messages.length === 0) {\n    return { success: false, error: 'No messages found.' };\n  }\n\n  try {\n    const client = createLLMClient();\n    const firstMsgs = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages.slice(0, 20));\n    const lastMsgs = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages.slice(-20));\n    const prompt = generateFacetOnlyPrompt(\n      session.project_name,\n      session.summary,\n      firstMsgs,\n      lastMsgs\n    );",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Extract facets only for a session that already has insights (backfill).\n * Uses the full conversation transcript for accurate friction attribution.\n * Falls back to truncation for sessions exceeding token limits.\n */\nexport async function extractFacetsOnly(\n  session: SessionData,\n  messages: SQLiteMessageRow[],\n  options?: { signal?: AbortSignal }\n): Promise<{ success: boolean; error?: string }> {\n  if (!isLLMConfigured()) {\n    return { success: false, error: 'LLM not configured.' };\n  }\n\n  if (messages.length === 0) {\n    return { success: false, error: 'No messages found.' };\n  }\n\n  try {\n    const client = createLLMClient();\n    let formattedMessages = formatMessagesForAnalysis(messages);\n\n    // Truncate if conversation exceeds token limits (same pattern as PQ analysis)\n    const estimatedTokens = client.estimateTokens(formattedMessages);\n    if (estimatedTokens > MAX_INPUT_TOKENS) {\n      const targetLength = Math.floor((MAX_INPUT_TOKENS / estimatedTokens) * formattedMessages.length * 0.8);\n      formattedMessages = formattedMessages.slice(0, targetLength) + '\\n\\n[... conversation truncated for analysis ...]';\n    }\n\n    const prompt = generateFacetOnlyPrompt(\n      session.project_name,\n      session.summary,\n      formattedMessages\n    );"
}

  "description": "Create pull request"
}

---

Now, here's the **implementation plan for the approved direction** (from the design doc):

## Implementation Steps — Final Approval Needed

### Step 1: Merge PR #139 (backfill full conversation fix) — ready now

### Step 2: Restructure Session Insights Prompt (next PR)
- Add 1-2 facet examples to the session insights prompt (~150 tokens) — model currently has zero examples for friction/pattern output
- Move classification guidance inline with schema definitions
- Trim insight examples from 2 to 1 (~350 tokens freed)
- Evaluate dropping `session_character` from LLM (deterministic detection exists in `titles.ts`)

### Step 3: Wire PQ Aggregates into Reflect (follow-up PR)
- Add PQ finding aggregation query to `shared-aggregation.ts` (~20 lines)
- Include PQ deficit counts in `reflect-prompts.ts` Friction & Wins payload (~10 lines)
- Enables cross-signal synthesis: friction categories correlated with PQ deficit categories

### Step 4: Add `--prompt-quality` flag to `reflect backfill` (follow-up PR)
- New endpoint `POST /api/facets/backfill-pq` — bulk re-run PQ analysis
- New CLI flag: `reflect backfill --prompt-quality`
- Finds sessions with insights but no/outdated PQ data

**Steps 2-4 are each independent PRs, done in order.** Want me to proceed with Step 2 after you merge #139?
